D(H_{i},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\iota}\,}D(H_{j},K)\longrightarrow D(H_{i}\cdot H_{j},K)\subset D(G,K)

to define (
1.8
).
The multiplication map of
G
induces a continuous homomorphism

C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i}\cdot H_{j},K)\overset{m^{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i}\times H_{j},K)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{j},K)

using
Proposition

1.2.14
(ii) and
Corollary

1.2.18
.
Taking the transpose of this homomorphism yields the continuous homomorphism

D(H_{i},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\iota}\,}D(H_{j},K)\cong D(H_{i},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}D(H_{j},K)\longrightarrow D(H_{i}\cdot H_{j},K)

by
[
67
, Prop. 17.6]
and
[
67
, Prop. 20.13]
.
This also shows that the convolution product is given by (
1.9
), and that it is jointly continuous if
G
is compact.
Moreover, for
g\in H_{i}
,
g^{\prime}\in H_{j}
, the linear form
\delta_{(g,g^{\prime})}
agrees with
\delta_{g}\otimes\delta_{g^{\prime}}
on
C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i},K){\,\otimes_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{j},K)\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i}\times H_{j},K)
.
Using that
C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{i},K){\,\otimes_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H_{j},K)
is a dense subspace, this implies that
\delta_{g}\ast\delta_{g^{\prime}}=\delta_{gg^{\prime}}
.
∎

Definition and Proposition 1.4.4
.

For a locally
L
-analytic Lie group
G
, the convolution product (
1.8
) endows
D(G,K)
with the structure of an associative, unital
K
-algebra called the
(locally analytic) distribution algebra
of
G
.
Its unit element is
\delta_{e}
where
e
is the identity element of
G
.
We also write
D(G)\vcentcolon=D(G,K)
when the coefficient field
K
is clear from the context.

Proof.

By the separate continuity of (
1.8
) it suffices to check the necessary properties of
D(G)
only for Dirac distributions.
But for those they directly follow from the respective properties of
G
as a group due to
\delta_{g}\ast\delta_{g^{\prime}}=\delta_{gg^{\prime}}
, for
g,g^{\prime}\in G
.
∎

Corollary 1.4.5

(cf.
[
58
, Proof of Prop. 3.5]
)
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group.
For
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)
and distributions
\mu,\nu\in D(G)
, the functions
G\rightarrow K
given by

\displaystyle g\longmapsto\nu\big[g^{\prime}\mapsto f(gg^{\prime})\big]\quad\text{and}\quad g^{\prime}\longmapsto\mu\big[g\mapsto f(gg^{\prime})\big]

are locally analytic, and we have the following identities reminiscent of Fubini’s theorem:

(1.10)

\displaystyle(\mu\ast\nu)(f)=\mu\big[g\mapsto\nu\big[g^{\prime}\mapsto f(gg^{\prime})\big]\big]=\nu\big[g^{\prime}\mapsto\mu\big[g\mapsto f(gg^{\prime})\big]\big].

Proof.

Assuming
\nu
is supported on the compact open subsets
H^{\prime}\subset G
, the first function restricted to some compact open subset
H\subset G
is the image of
f
under

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\cdot H^{\prime},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\times H^{\prime},K)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},K))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu_{\ast}}

Analogously, one shows that the second function is locally analytic.
Now let
\mu
be supported on the compact open subset
H\subset G
.
Then the statement of (
1.10
) follows from the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\cdot H^{\prime},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\times H^{\prime},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},K))\overset{\nu_{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}

and the analogous diagram for
\nu\circ\mu_{\ast}
.
∎

The locally
L
-analytic anti-automorphism
{\mathrm{inv}}\colon G\rightarrow G,\,g\mapsto g^{-1},
induces by functoriality an automorphism of locally convex
K
-vector spaces

{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f\circ{\mathrm{inv}}.

Hence we obtain an automorphism of locally convex
K
-vector spaces

(1.11)

D(G)\longrightarrow D(G)\,,\quad\mu\longmapsto\dot{\mu}\vcentcolon=\mu\circ{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}=\big[f\mapsto\mu(f\circ{\mathrm{inv}})\big].

Lemma 1.4.6
.

For
\mu,\nu\in D(G)
, we have that

(1.12)

\left(\mu\ast\nu\right)\dot{}=\dot{\nu}\ast\dot{\mu}.

Proof.

We may assume that
\mu
and
\nu
are supported on compact open subsets
H
and
H^{\prime}
of
G
respectively.
Then the claim follows from the commutativity of the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime-1},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{-1},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}\big((H\cdot H^{\prime})^{-1},K\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\cdot H^{\prime},K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},K)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}{\,\otimes\,}{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nu\otimes\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{swap}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\nu}

∎

Proposition 1.4.7

(cf.
[
74
, Thm. 2.2]
)
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold and
V
a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
There exists a unique continuous
K
-linear integration map

(1.13)

I\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big)

such that
I(f)(\delta_{x})=f(x)
, for all
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)
and
x\in X
.
Moreover, this map is natural in
X
and
V
, and injective.

If
V
is of LB-type, i.e.
V=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}
, for a sequence
V_{0}\subset V_{1}\subset\ldots\subset V
of BH-subspaces, then (
1.13
) is an isomorphism of
K
-vector spaces with inverse

I^{-1}\colon{\mathcal{L}}\big(D(X,K),V\big)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\,,\quad T\longmapsto\big[x\mapsto T(\delta_{x})\big].

Proof.

First note that, for
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)
, the condition
I(f)(\delta_{x})=f(x)
, for all
x\in X
, determines
I(f)
uniquely by the density of the subspace generated by the Dirac distributions of
X
.

For the existence of
I
take a countable disjoint covering
X=\bigcup_{i\in I}X_{i}
by open compact subsets.
Then
Proposition

1.2.13
and
Proposition

1.4.2
(iii) give topological isomorphisms
C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\cong\prod_{i\in I}C^{\mathrm{la}}(X_{i},V)
and
D(X,K)\cong\bigoplus_{i\in I}D(X_{i},K)
respectively, with the
D(X_{i},K)
being
K
-Fréchet spaces.
By
Lemma

A.9
we have the topological isomorphism

{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{i\in I}{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X_{i},K),V\big)\,,\quad F\longmapsto\big(F{\!\,\mid_{D(X_{i},K)}}\big)_{i\in I}.

This way, we may reduce to the case that
X
is compact.

Here we first assume that
V
is a
K
-Banach space.
As
C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,K)
is of compact type, we have a continuous linear bijection
C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V
by
Proposition

A.7
.
Together with
[
67
, Cor. 18.8]
this gives the continuous linear bijection

(1.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)}
\displaystyle C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f({\,\_\,})\,v}
\displaystyle f\otimes v
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big[\mu\mapsto\mu(f)\,v\big]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

which satisfies
I(f)(\delta_{x})=f(x)
.

If
V=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}
is of LB-type then every BH-subspace of
V
factors over some
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}
by
[
11
, I. §3.3 Prop. 1]
.
Hence the compactness of
X
implies by
Proposition

1.2.15
(i) that

C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}).

Furthermore, the continuous injections
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}\hookrightarrow V
induce continuous homomorphisms

{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big).

These in turn give rise to a continuous homomorphism

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big)

which is bijective by
[
11
, I. §3.3 Prop. 1]
.
Taking the direct limit over the continuous linear bijections (
1.14
), for all
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}
, we now arrive at the continuous linear bijection

I\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V})\longrightarrow\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V}\big)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big(D(X,K),V\big).

For the case of a general Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space
V
, we observe that by the compactness of
X
, every locally analytic
V
-valued function on
X
factors over some BH-subspace of
V
.
Therefore even in this case, we obtain the injective
K
-linear map
I
.
∎

Corollary 1.4.8
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold and
V\neq\{0\}
a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
Then there is a natural, separately continuous, non-degenerate
K
-bilinear pairing

(1.15)

D(X,K)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)\longrightarrow V\,,\quad(\mu,f)\longmapsto\mu(f)\vcentcolon=I(f)(\mu).

This pairing is induced by the duality between
D(X,K)
and
C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,K)
in the sense that, for compact open
U\subset X
and a BH-subspace
E\subset V
, the restriction of the pairing (
1.15
) to
D(U,K)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
is given by tensoring the duality pairing
D(U,K)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,K)\rightarrow K
with
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
:

(1.16)

D(U,K)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}\longrightarrow\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}\,,\quad(\mu,f\otimes v)\longmapsto\mu(f)\,v,

using
Proposition

A.7
.

Proof.

It is clear that the pairing (
1.15
) defined by
I
is natural,
K
-bilinear, and separately continuous.
The claimed compatibility with the duality pairing between
D(X,K)
and
C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,K)
follows from the construction of
I
in (
1.14
).

To show the non-degeneracy, fix a distribution
\mu\in D(X,K)
and assume that
\mu(f)=0
, for all
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)
.
Let
\mu
be supported on a compact open subset
U\subset X
.
Moreover, we find
v\in V
,
v\neq 0
, contained in some BH-subspace
E\subset V
.
For all
h\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,K)
, we then have
\mu(h)\,v=\mu(h\otimes v)=0
.
Therefore, the non-degeneracy of the duality pairing between
D(U,K)
and
C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,K)
implies that
\mu=0
.
On the other hand, consider
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(X,V)
such that
\mu(f)=0
, for all
\mu\in D(X,K)
.
Then we have
f(x)=I(f)(\delta_{x})=0
, for all Dirac distributions
\delta_{x}
, and hence
f=0
.
∎

Proposition 1.4.9

(
[
74
, Prop. 3.2]
)
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group, and
V
a locally analytic
G
-representation with orbit maps
\rho_{v}\colon G\rightarrow V
, for
v\in V
.
The
K
-bilinear map

(1.17)

D(G)\times V\longrightarrow V\,,\quad(\mu,v)\longmapsto\mu\ast v\vcentcolon=I(\rho_{v})(\mu),

is separately continuous, and
V
becomes a
D(G)
-module this way.
If
G
is compact and
V
a
K
-Fréchet space, (
1.17
) even is jointly continuous.

The
K
-bilinear map

(1.18)

D(G)\times V^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow V^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad(\mu,\ell)\longmapsto\mu\ast\ell\vcentcolon=\big[v\mapsto\ell(\dot{\mu}\ast v)\big],

given by the
D(G)
-action contragredient to (
1.17
) is separately continuous, and
V^{\prime}_{b}
becomes a
D(G)
-module this way.
If
G
is compact and
V^{\prime}_{b}
a
K
-Fréchet space, e.g. if
V
is of compact type, then (
1.18
) even is jointly continuous.

In particular, we have
\delta_{g}\ast v=g.v
, and
\delta_{g}\ast\ell=g.\ell
, for all
g\in G
,
v\in V
,
\ell\in V^{\prime}_{b}
, where
g.\ell=\ell(g^{-1}.{\,\_\,})
denotes the contragredient
G
-action.

Proof.

The
K
-bilinearity of (
1.17
) and its continuity in
D(G)
are clear.
Now fix a distribution
\mu\in D(G)
.
We may assume that
\mu\in D(H,K)
, for some compact open subset
H\subset G
.
By
Proposition

1.4.2
(iv), as
D(H,K)
is metrizable,
\mu
is the limit of a sequence
(\mu_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
in
D(H,K)
where the
\mu_{n}
are linear combinations of Dirac distributions.
But a Dirac distribution
\delta_{g}
,
g\in H
, acts by the continuous endomorphism
v\mapsto g.v
on
V
.
Hence the
\mu_{n}
act by continuous endomorphisms as well.
As these continuous endomorphisms of
V
converge to the endomorphism induced by
\mu
pointwise, it follows from a version of the Banach-Steinhaus theorem
[
11
, III. §4.2, Cor. 2]
that the endomorphism induced by
\mu
is continuous.

To show that (
1.17
) endows
V
with the structure of a
D(G)
-module, we have to see that
\delta_{e}\ast v=v
, for the identity element
e
of
G
, and
\mu\ast(\nu\ast v)=(\mu\ast\nu)\ast v
, for all
v\in V
,
\mu,\nu\in D(G)
.
But this holds for Dirac distributions, and hence for general elements of
D(G)
by continuity.

For (ii), note that the homomorphism
D(G)\rightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,V)
induced from (
1.17
) is continuous by
[
11
, III. §5.3, Prop. 6]
as
D(G)
is reflexive and therefore barrelled
[
67
, Lemma 15.4]
.
Moreover, taking the transpose gives a topological embedding
{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,V)\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V^{\prime}_{b},V^{\prime}_{b})
by
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.36]
.
Combining this with (
1.11
) yields

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,V)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V^{\prime}_{b},V^{\prime}_{b})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dot{\mu}}

which gives the separately continuous
K
-bilinear pairing (
1.18
).
To see that this defines a
D(G)
-module structure on
V^{\prime}_{b}
, one again considers Dirac distributions first and then extends to general elements of
D(G)
by continuity.

If
G
is compact and
V
or
V^{\prime}_{b}
is a Fréchet space, the joint continuity of (
1.17
) and (
1.18
) follows from
[
11
, III. §5.2 Cor. 1]
because
D(G)
is a
K
-Fréchet space in this case.
∎

Proposition 1.4.10

(cf.
[
74
, §3]
)
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group.
Associating a
D(G)
-module structure via (
1.17
) gives an equivalence of categories

(1.19)

\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\small locally analytic $G$-representations}\\
\text{\small on locally convex $K$-vector spaces}\\
\text{\small of LB-type with continuous}\\
\text{\small$G$-equivariant homomorphisms}\end{subarray}\right)\longrightarrow\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\small separately continuous $D(G)$-modules}\\
\text{\small on locally convex $K$-vector spaces}\\
\text{\small of LB-type with continuous}\\
\text{\small$D(G)$-module maps}\end{subarray}\right).

Passing to the strong dual and associating the
D(G)
-module structure of (
1.18
) gives an anti-equivalence of categories

(1.20)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\small locally analytic $G$-representations}\\
\text{\small on locally convex $K$-vector spaces}\\
\text{\small of compact type with continuous}\\
\text{\small$G$-equivariant homomorphisms}\end{subarray}\right)&\longrightarrow\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\text{\small separately continuous $D(G)$-modules}\\
\text{\small on nuclear $K$-Fr\'{e}chet spaces}\\
\text{\small with continuous $D(G)$-module maps}\end{subarray}\right)\\
\left[f\colon V\rightarrow W\right]&\longmapsto\left[f^{t}\colon W^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow V^{\prime}_{b}\right].\end{split}

If
G
is compact, the latter category already is equal to the category of continuous
D(G)
-modules on nuclear
K
-Fréchet spaces with continuous
D(G)
-module homomorphisms.

Proof.

For a continuous
G
-equivariant homomorphism
f\colon V\rightarrow W
, we immediately have
\delta_{g}\ast f(v)=f(\delta_{g}\ast v)
, for all
g\in G
,
v\in V
.
Hence it follows by continuity and density of the space of Dirac distributions that
f
is a
D(G)
-module homomorphism.
On the other hand, if
V
is a separately continuous
D(G)
-module and of LB-type, we can define a
G
-action with locally analytic orbit maps
\rho_{v}\vcentcolon=I^{-1}(\mu\mapsto\mu\ast v)
, for
v\in V
, using
Proposition

1.4.7
(ii).
Then
g\in G
acts by the endomorphism
V\rightarrow V
,
v\mapsto\delta_{g}\ast v
, which therefore is continuous.
One readily checks that the functor defined this way is a quasi-inverse to (
1.19
).

For the statement of (ii), note that by (i) the first category is equivalent to the category of separately continuous
D(G)
-modules on locally convex
K
-vector spaces of compact type with continuous
D(G)
-module maps.
Now
Proposition

A.5
and
Proposition

1.4.9
(ii) show that the functor (
1.20
) is well defined and essentially surjective.
Moreover, for locally convex
K
-vector spaces
V
and
W
of compact type, one readily checks that the homomorphism

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,W)}
\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(W^{\prime}_{b},V^{\prime}_{b})
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{L}}_{b}\big((V^{\prime}_{b})^{\prime}_{b},(W^{\prime}_{b})^{\prime}_{b}\big)\cong{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,W),}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\displaystyle f^{t}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{t})^{t},}

using the reflexivity of
V
and
W

[
67
, Prop. 16.10 (i)]
, is in fact the identity.
Combined with a similar argument for
{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(W^{\prime}_{b},V^{\prime}_{b})
we conclude that the natural map induced by taking the transpose is a topological isomorphism

\par{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(V,W)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(W^{\prime}_{b},V^{\prime}_{b})\,,\quad f\longmapsto f^{t}.

Furthermore, one computes that
f
is a homomorphism of
D(G)
-modules if and only if
f^{t}
is.
Therefore, (
1.20
) is an anti-equivalence of categories.
Finally, if
G
is compact, a separately continuous
D(G)
-module structure on a
K
-Fréchet space is jointly continuous by
[
11
, III. §5.2 Cor. 1]
, like before.
∎

1.5.
Locally Analytic Induction

We keep the setting that
K
is a spherically complete non-archimedean field and
L\subset K
a locally compact complete subfield.
We will now recall the notion of locally analytic induction from
[
30
, Kap. 4]
and make some easy comparisons to the “finite” induction of locally analytic representation.

Definition 1.5.1

(
[
30
, §4.1]
)
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group,
H\subset G
a locally
L
-analytic subgroup, and
V
a locally analytic
H
-representation.
We define the subspace

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)\vcentcolon=\left\{f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\,\middle|\,\forall g\in G,h\in H:f(gh)=h^{-1}.f(g)\right\}\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)

and consider it with the left regular
G
-action.
We note that the continuity of the evaluation homomorphisms and the action of
H
on
V
imply that this subspace is closed.

Proposition 1.5.2

(cf.
[
30
, Satz 4.1.5]
)
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group and
H\subset G
a locally
L
-analytic subgroup.
Moreover, let
V
be a locally analytic
H
-representation.

(cf.
[
26
, §2.1]
)

If
V
is of compact type and there exists a compact open subgroup
G_{0}\subset G
such that
G=G_{0}\cdot H
, then
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
is a locally analytic
G
-representation of compact type.

(cf.
[
30
, Satz 4.3.1]
)

If
V
is a
K
-Banach space and
G/H
is compact, then
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
is a locally analytic
G
-representation and any section of the projection map
G\rightarrow G/H
induces a topological isomorphism
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(G/H,V)
.

Proof.

In both cases it follows from the functoriality of
Proposition

1.2.14
(ii) that
G
acts on
{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)
by continuous endomorphisms.
In the first case, we set
H_{0}\vcentcolon=G_{0}\cap H
, for such
G_{0}\subset G
.
Arguing analogously to
[
26
, §2.1]
we may view
V
as a locally analytic
H_{0}
-representation and have an identification

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)

\displaystyle\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G_{0}}_{H_{0}}(V)\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V),

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\longmapsto f{\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}}

of (abstract)
G_{0}
-representations on locally convex
K
-vector spaces.
Because
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
is of compact type (
Proposition

1.2.15
(iii)), the closed subspace
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
is so as well by
Proposition

A.4
. Moreover, the orbit maps of the
G_{0}
-action on
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G_{0}}_{H_{0}}(V)
are locally analytic by
[
30
, Satz 4.1.5]
since
G_{0}/H_{0}
is compact.
It follows from
Proposition

1.3.9
that
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
is a locally analytic
G
-representation.

In the second case, the orbit maps of the
G
-action on
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
again are locally analytic by
[
30
, Satz 4.1.5]
as
G/H
is assumed to be compact.
The topological isomorphism
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(G/H,V)
, for any section of
G\rightarrow G/H
, is the content of
[
30
, Satz 4.3.1]
.
It follows that
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
is barrelled (see
Corollary

1.2.12
) and therefore a locally analytic
G
-representation. ∎

Lemma 1.5.3

(see discussion after
[
27
, Thm. 3.6.12]
)
.

Let
V
be a locally analytic representation of a locally
L
-analytic Lie group
G
with orbit maps
\rho_{v}\colon G\rightarrow V
, for
v\in V
.
If
V
is an LF-space (i.e.
V
is topologically isomorphic to the inductive limit of a sequence of
K
-Fréchet spaces) then the orbit homomorphism

o\colon V\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\,,\quad v\longmapsto\rho_{v},

is continuous.

Proof.

Let
H\subset G
be a compact open subgroup so that
G=\bigcup_{i\in I}Hg_{i}
is a disjoint covering.
Under the topological isomorphism from
Proposition

1.2.13
, the homomorphism
o
coincides with the map

\displaystyle V\longrightarrow\prod_{i\in I}C^{\mathrm{la}}(Hg_{i},V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\,,\quad v\longmapsto\big(\rho_{v}{\!\,\mid_{Hg_{i}}}\big)_{i\in I}.

Hence it suffices to show that
o_{i}\colon V\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(Hg_{i},V)
,
v\mapsto\rho_{v}{\!\,\mid_{Hg_{i}}}
, is continuous, for all
i\in I
.

We fix
i\in I
and consider the graph
\Gamma_{o^{\prime}}\subset V\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},V)
, for
H^{\prime}\vcentcolon=Hg_{i}
.
One readily computes that
\Gamma_{o^{\prime}}
is precisely the kernel of the continuous homomorphism

V\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},V)\longrightarrow\prod_{h\in H}V\,,\quad(v,f)\longmapsto\big((hg_{i}).v-f(hg_{i})\big)_{h\in H}.

Therefore
\Gamma_{o^{\prime}}\subset V\times C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},V)
is closed, and we conclude by a version of the closed graph theorem
[
11
, II. §4.6 Prop. 10]
that
o^{\prime}
is continuous.
For this we remark that in particular
V
is of LF-type (see
[
27
, p. 15]
) so that
C^{\mathrm{la}}(H^{\prime},V)
is of LF-type by
Proposition

1.2.15
(ii).
∎

When
H
is a subgroup of finite index in
G
, it suggest itself to consider the “finite” induction of a
H
-representation
V

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(V)\vcentcolon=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet V

where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
are coset representatives of
G/H
.
Here the
G
-action on
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(V)
is defined via

g.\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\bigg)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{j(i)}\bullet h_{i}.v_{i},

for
g\in G
with
gg_{i}=g_{j(i)}h_{i}
,
j(i)\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
,
h_{i}\in H
.
We can compare this to the locally analytic induction.

Proposition 1.5.4
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group and
H\subset G
a locally
L
-analytic subgroup of finite index.
Let
V
be a locally analytic
H
-representation which is an LF-space.
Then there is a
G
-equivariant, topological isomorphism

(1.21)

{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(V)\,,\quad f\longmapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet f(g_{i}),

where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
are coset representatives of
G/H
.

Proof.

We first note that the homomorphism (
1.21
) is
G
-equivariant; it is continuous because the evaluation homomorphisms
{\mathrm{ev}}_{g_{i}}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\rightarrow V
are.
Then we consider

{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(V)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)\,,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\longmapsto\big[g\mapsto h^{-1}.v_{i}\quad\text{, for $g=g_{i}h$ with $h\in H$}\big],

which is an inverse to (
1.21
).
Using
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}C^{\mathrm{la}}(g_{i}H,V)
this homomorphism is the direct sum of the homomorphisms

g_{i}\bullet V\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(g_{i}H,V)\cap{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)\,,\quad v\longmapsto\big[g_{i}h\mapsto h^{-1}.v\quad\text{, for $h\in H$}\big].

These in turn each can be identified with
{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}\circ o\colon V\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,V)
which is continuous by
Lemma

1.5.3
.
∎

Remarks 1.5.5
.

Let
G
be a group and
H\subset G
a subgroup of finite index.
Let
V
be a locally convex
K
-vector space which also is an (abstract)
H
-representation.
Then we have a
G
-equivariant, topological isomorphism
{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(V)^{\prime}_{b}\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{G}(V^{\prime}_{b})
via

\bigg(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet V\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet V^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad\ell\longmapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet\big[v\mapsto\ell(g_{i}\bullet v)\big],

where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
are coset representatives of
G/H
.

We also have the following version of a push-pull formula (projection formula):
Let
V
and
W
be locally convex
K
-vector space such that
V
is an (abstract)
G
-representation and
W
an (abstract)
H
-representation.
Then there exists a
G
-equivariant, topological isomorphism

V{\,\otimes_{K}\,}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}(W)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G}_{H}\big(V{\!\,\mid_{H}}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}W\big)\,,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}\otimes g_{i}\bullet w_{i}\longmapsto\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\bullet(g_{i}^{-1}.v_{i}\otimes w_{i})

when the tensor products either both carry the projective or inductive tensor product topology.
Again
g_{1},\ldots,g_{n}
denote coset representatives of
G/H
.

In view of the anti-equivalence from
Proposition

1.4.10
(ii), the locally analytic induction can also be expressed in terms of taking tensor products with locally analytic distribution algebras.
To this end, we need the following from
[
46
, Rmk. 1.2.11]
and
[
27
, §1.2]
.

Definition 1.5.6
.

By a
separately continuous locally convex
K
-algebra
we mean a locally convex
K
-vector space
A
which carries the structure of a
K
-algebra such that the multiplication map
A\times A\rightarrow A
is separately continuous.
If the multiplication map even is jointly continuous, we simply call
A
a
locally convex
K
-algebra
.

If in addition
A
is a
K
-Fréchet space, we call
A
a
K
-Fréchet algebra
.
We remark that the multiplication map of such an algebra is jointly continuous automatically
[
11
, III. §5.2 Cor. 1]
.

Let
A
be a separately continuous locally convex
K
-algebra and
M
a locally convex
K
-vector space.
We call
M
a
separately continuous locally convex (left)
A
-module
if
M
is a left
A
-module and the scalar multiplication map is separately continuous.
If
A
is a locally convex
K
-algebra and the scalar multiplication map is jointly continuous, we call
M
a
locally convex (left)
A
-module
.

If
M
is a separately continuous locally convex
A
-module, for a
K
-Fréchet algebra
A
, and a
K
-Fréchet space itself, we call
M
an
A
-Fréchet module
.
Again the scalar multiplication of such
M
is jointly continuous automatically.

Lemma 1.5.7

(
[
27
, Lemma 1.2.3]
)
.

Let
A\rightarrow B
be a continuous homomorphism of locally convex
K
-algebras, and
M
a locally convex
A
-module.
Then there is an isomorphism of
B
-modules

B{\,\otimes_{A}\,}M\cong\big(B{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}M\big)\big/M^{\prime},

where
M^{\prime}
is the
B
-submodule generated by
ba\otimes m-b\otimes am
, for
b\in B
,
a\in A
,
m\in M
.
With the induced quotient topology
B{\,\otimes_{A}\,}M
becomes a locally convex
B
-module.

Definition 1.5.8
.

In the situation of the above lemma, we let
B{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M
denote the
B
-module
B{\,\otimes_{A}\,}M
with this quotient topology.
Moreover, we write
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\pi}\,}M
for its Hausdorff completion.

Remark 1.5.9
.

Note that
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\pi}\,}M
again is a locally convex
B
-module.
If
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}M
is hereditarily complete, i.e. all its Hausdorff quotients are complete
[
27
, Def. 1.1.39]
, then by
[
13
, Cor. 2.2]
completing preserves the strict exactness of

0\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\longrightarrow B{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}M\longrightarrow B{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M\longrightarrow 0.

We thus have
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\pi}\,}M\cong\big(B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}M\big)\big/\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
where
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{M}
denotes the closure of
M^{\prime}
in
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}M
.
The above condition on
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}M
is fulfilled for example when
B
and
M
both are
K
-Fréchet spaces (see discussion after
[
67
, Prop. 17.6]
) or both are of compact type (see
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.32 (i)]
) by the comment after
[
27
, Def. 1.1.39]
.

Similarly if
A\rightarrow B
is a continuous homomorphism of separately continuous locally convex
K
-algebras and
M
is a separately continuous locally convex
A
-module, then
B{\,\otimes_{A}\,}M
becomes a separately continuous locally convex
B
-module when given the quotient topology of
B{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M
(cf.
[
46
, Rmk. 1.2.11]
).
We write
B{\,\otimes_{A,\iota}\,}M
in this case.
Furthermore, we let
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{A,\iota}\,}M
denote its Hausdorff completion.

Remark 1.5.10
.

In the case that
A\rightarrow B
is a continuous homomorphism of
K
-Fréchet algebras and
M
is an
A
-Fréchet module, the projective and inductive tensor product topology on
B{\,\otimes_{K}\,}M
agree
[
67
, Prop. 17.6]
.
Consequently we then simply write
B{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{A}\,}M
(and
B{\,\otimes_{A}\,}M
) to denote the
B
-Fréchet module (respectively, the locally convex
B
-module).

Lemma 1.5.11
.

For a locally convex (respectively, separately continuous locally convex) unital
K
-algebra
A
and a locally convex (respectively, separately continuous locally convex)
A
-module
M
, there is a topological isomorphism of locally convex
A
-modules

A{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}M\,,\quad a\otimes m\longmapsto am,

(respectively, of separately continuous locally convex
A
-modules
A{\,\otimes_{A,\iota}\,}M\cong M
).

Let
A
and
B
be locally convex
K
-algebras, and
L
,
M
and
N
a locally convex right
A
-module, a locally convex
A
-
B
-bi-module and a locally convex left
B
-module respectively.
Then there is a canonical topological isomorphism

\big(L{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{B,\pi}\,}N\cong L{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{B,\pi}\,}N\big).

For separately continuous locally convex
K
-algebras and separately continuous locally convex modules, the analogous assertion holds with respect to the inductive tensor product topologies instead.

Proof.

In (i), the homomorphisms

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\,\otimes_{A,\iota}\,}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M}

given by
m\mapsto 1\otimes m
are continuous by the definition of the inductive tensor product topology (see
[
67
, §17 A.]
), and constitute inverses to the respective claimed isomorphisms.

For (ii), it is a classical result that the tensor product over a (commutative) ring is associative.
In particular this holds for lattices in
L
,
M
and
N
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
so that

\varphi\colon\big(L{\,\otimes_{K}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{K}\,}N\longrightarrow L{\,\otimes_{K}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{K}\,}N\big)

even is a topological isomorphism with respect to the projective tensor product topologies.
(These are defined by the tensor product of such lattices over
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
, see
[
67
, §17 B.]
).
We can now pass to the quotients

\displaystyle\pi_{\ell}\colon

\displaystyle\big(L{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}N\longrightarrow\big(L{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}N\longrightarrow\big(L{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{B,\pi}\,}N,

\displaystyle\pi_{r}\colon

\displaystyle L{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}N\big)\longrightarrow L{\,\otimes_{K,\pi}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{B,\pi}\,}N\big)\longrightarrow L{\,\otimes_{A,\pi}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{B,\pi}\,}N\big).

Then
\pi_{r}\circ\varphi
factors over
\pi_{\ell}
, and
\pi_{\ell}\circ\varphi^{-1}
factors over
\pi_{r}
yielding the sought topological isomorphism.

In the case of only separately continuous locally convex
K
-algebras and modules, we recall that the inductive tensor product topology on the tensor product
V{\,\otimes_{K}\,}W
of two locally convex
K
-vector spaces
V
and
W
is the final locally convex topology with respect to the homomorphisms

\displaystyle{\,\_\,}\otimes w

\displaystyle\colon V

\displaystyle\longrightarrow V{\,\otimes_{K}\,}W\,,\quad v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longmapsto v^{\prime}\otimes w,

\displaystyle v\otimes{\,\_\,}

\displaystyle\colon W

\displaystyle\longrightarrow V{\,\otimes_{K}\,}W\,,\quad w^{\prime}

\displaystyle\longmapsto v\otimes w^{\prime},

for all
v\in V
,
w\in W
.
For fixed
v=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\ell_{i}\otimes m_{i}\in L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M
, it then follows from the commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}N\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{i}\otimes{\,\_\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\ell_{i}\otimes m_{i})\otimes{\,\_\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ell_{i}\otimes{\,\_\,}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

that
\varphi\circ(v\otimes{\,\_\,})
is continuous.
In turn for fixed
n\in N
, the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\big){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}\big(M{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}N\big)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L{\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}({\,\_\,}\otimes n)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\,\_\,}\otimes n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}

shows that
\varphi\circ({\,\_\,}\otimes n)
is continuous as well.
Therefore
[
67
, Lemma 5.1 (i)]
implies that
\varphi
is continuous.
Similarly one deduces that
\varphi^{-1}
is continuous so that
\varphi
is a topological isomorphism with respect to the inductive tensor product topologies.
One then argues like in the preceding case.
∎

Proposition 1.5.12

(cf.
[
47
, §5]
)
.

Let
G
be a compact locally
L
-analytic Lie group with a locally
L
-analytic subgroup
H\subset G
, and let
V
be a locally analytic
H
-representation of compact type.
Then there is a canonical topological isomorphism of
D(G)
-modules

\big({\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)\big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H)}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.

Proof.

By the definition of
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)
and using
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V
, we have the exact sequence

(1.22)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{H}(V)\overset{\iota}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)

\displaystyle\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,V

\displaystyle\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{g\in G,h\in H}V

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\otimes v

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(f(gh)\,v-f(g)\,h^{-1}.v\big)_{g,h}

where
\iota
is strict.
We want to consider the complex obtained by taking the strong dual of (
1.22
).
The homomorphisms of this dual complex are continuous by
[
67
, Rmk. 16.1]
.

By
[
67
, Prop. 9.11]
we have a topological isomorphism

\bigg(\prod_{g\in G,h\in H}V\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\bigoplus_{g\in G,h\in H}V^{\prime}_{b}.

Under this isomorphism the transpose of
\psi
is given by

\displaystyle\psi^{t}\colon\bigoplus_{g\in G,h\in H}V^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b},

\displaystyle\sum\ell_{g,h}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\bigg[f\otimes v\mapsto\sum f(gh)\,\ell_{g,h}(v)-f(g)\,\ell_{g,h}(h^{-1}.v)\bigg].

There also is the topological isomorphism

D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad\delta\otimes\ell\longmapsto\big[f\otimes v\mapsto\delta(f)\,\ell(v)\big],

by
[
67
, Prop. 20.13]
and
[
67
, Cor. 20.14]
.
Because we have
f(g)\,\ell(h^{-1}.v)=\delta_{g}(f)\,(h.\ell)(v)
and
f(gh)=(\delta_{g}\ast\delta_{h})(f)
, the complex of the strong duals of (
1.22
) is

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{g\in G,h\in H}V^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\overset{\psi^{t}}{\longrightarrow}D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\iota^{t}}{\longrightarrow}\big({\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)\big)^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\sum\ell_{g,h}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\delta_{g}\ast\delta_{h}\otimes\ell_{g,h}-\delta_{g}\otimes h.\ell_{g,h}.

Since
\iota
is a closed embedding it follows from the Hahn-Banach theorem
[
67
, Cor. 9.4]
that
\iota^{t}
is surjective, and from the open mapping theorem
[
67
, Prop. 8.6]
that
\iota^{t}
is strict.
Furthermore, we have
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})={\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}
by
[
11
, IV. §4.1 Prop. 2]
where

{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}\vcentcolon=\left\{\ell\in D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\,\middle|\,\forall v\in{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota):\ell(v)=0\right\}.

Then it follows from the algebraic exactness of (
1.22
) that
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}={\mathrm{Ker}}(\psi)^{\perp}
which implies that
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\psi)^{\perp}\subset\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
by
Lemma

A.11
.
Because
{\mathrm{Im}}(\psi^{t})\subset{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})
and
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})
is closed, we have
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
.
But
{\mathrm{Im}}(\psi^{t})
is generated by the elements

\delta_{g}\ast\delta_{h}\otimes\ell-\delta_{g}\ast h.\ell\quad\text{, for $g\in G$, $h\in H$, $\ell\in V^{\prime}_{b}$.}

Therefore
Remark

1.5.9
together with the density of the Dirac distributions yields the topological isomorphism

\big({\mathrm{Ind}}_{H}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}(V)\big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong\big(D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\big)/\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}\cong D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D(H)}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.

As
\iota^{t}
is
D(G)
-linear with respect to the
D(G)
-action on the first component of
D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}
via left multiplication, we see that the above isomorphism is an isomorphism of
D(G)
-modules. ∎

1.6.
The Hyperalgebra

In this section
K
continues to be a spherically complete non-archimedean field with a locally compact complete subfield
L\subset K
.
We recapitulate the concept of germs of locally analytic functions and investigate certain subalgebras of the dual space of these following
[
30
, §2.3]
and
[
46
, §1.2]
.

Definition 1.6.1
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold, and
V
a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
For
x\in X
, we define the
space of germs of locally analytic functions on
X
with values in
V
at
x
as the inductive limit over all open neighbourhoods
U\subset X
of
x

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\vcentcolon=\varinjlim_{x\in U\subset X}C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)

with respect to the canonical restriction homomorphisms and endowed with the inductive limit topology.

Lemma 1.6.2

(cf.
[
30
, §2.3.1]
)
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold, and
V
a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
For every open subset
U\subset X
with
x\in U
, the canonical map

C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)

is a strict epimorphism.

We have a canonical topological isomorphism

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\cong\varinjlim_{(U,E)}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})

where the latter inductive limit is taken over all pairs of analytic charts
\varphi\colon U\rightarrow L^{m}
and BH-subspaces
E\subset V
such that
x\in U
.
These are partially ordered via

(U,E)\geq(W,F)\,\vcentcolon\Longleftrightarrow\,\text{$U\subset W$ and $F\subset E$.}

In particular, the
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
are BH-subspaces.

Proof.

For (i) note that the restriction map
C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(U^{\prime},V)
, for open subsets
U^{\prime}\subset U
of
X
, is a strict epimorphism as it is given by the projection

C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\cong\prod_{i\in I}C^{\mathrm{la}}(U_{i},V)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(U^{\prime},V),

for a suitable disjoint covering
U=\bigcup_{i\in I}U_{i}
by open subsets such that
U^{\prime}\in\left\{U_{i}\right\}_{i\in I}
.
For a fixed open subset
U\subset X
with
x\in U
, the canonical homomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is the inductive limit over these restriction maps, for
U^{\prime}\subset U
with
x\in U^{\prime}
.
Because it is the colimit over the respective cokernels,
C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is a strict epimorphism itself.

For (ii), we have by definition

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)=\varinjlim_{x\in U\subset X}\Big(\varinjlim_{{\mathcal{I}}}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathcal{I}}(U,V)\Big)

where the “inner” inductive limit is taken over all
V
-indices
{\mathcal{I}}
of
U
.
The latter is topologically isomorphic to the inductive limit indexed by the directed set

\Phi\vcentcolon=\big\{(U,{\mathcal{I}})\,\big|\,\text{$U\subset X$ open with $x\in U$, ${\mathcal{I}}$ a $V$-index of $U$}\big\}

endowed with the following preorder:
Let
(U,{\mathcal{I}})
,
(W,{\mathcal{J}})
be elements of
\Phi
, with

{\mathcal{I}}=\big(\varphi_{i}\colon U_{i}\rightarrow L^{m_{i}},{\underline{r}}_{i},E_{i}\big)_{i\in I}\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\mathcal{J}}=\big(\psi_{j}\colon W_{j}\rightarrow L^{n_{j}},{\underline{s}}_{j},F_{j}\big)_{j\in J}.

Then we set
(U,{\mathcal{I}})\geq(W,{\mathcal{J}})
if
U\subset W
and the relation from the proof of
Proposition

1.2.14
(ii) between
{\mathcal{I}}
and
{\mathcal{J}}
for the embedding
U\hookrightarrow W
holds:
For every
i\in I
, there exists
j\in J
such that

U_{i}\subset W_{j}\cap U
, i.e. the covering of
{\mathcal{I}}
is a refinement of the covering
U=\bigcup_{j\in J}W_{j}\cap U
,

there exist
a_{i}\in\varphi_{i}(U_{i})
and
g_{i,j}=(g_{i,j,k})_{k=1,\ldots,n_{j}}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{\underline{r}}_{i}}(L^{m_{i}},L^{n_{j}})
such that

\lVert{g_{i,j,k}-g_{i,j,k}(0)}\rVert_{{\underline{r}}_{i}}\leq s_{j,k}\quad\text{, for all $k=1,\ldots,n_{j}$,}

and
\psi_{j}\circ\varphi_{i}^{-1}(x)=g_{i,j}(x-a_{i})
, for all
x\in\varphi_{i}(U_{i})=B_{{\underline{r}}_{i}}^{m_{i}}(a_{i})
,

F_{j}\subset E_{i}
.

Now consider the subset
\Psi\subset\Phi
of those
(U,{\mathcal{I}})
for which the covering of
{\mathcal{I}}
only consists of
U
itself.
This subset is cofinal in
\Phi
:
For
(U,{\mathcal{I}})\in\Phi
, let
i_{0}\in I
such that
x\in U_{i_{0}}
.
Then

(U_{i_{0}},{\mathcal{I}}_{0})\vcentcolon=\Big(U_{i_{0}},\big(\varphi_{i_{0}}\colon U_{i_{0}}\rightarrow L^{m_{i_{0}}},{\underline{r}}_{i_{0}},E_{i_{0}}\big)\Big)\in\Psi,

and
(U_{i_{0}},{\mathcal{I}}_{0})\geq(U,{\mathcal{I}})
.
Hence we conclude that

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\cong\varinjlim_{(U,{\mathcal{I}})\in\Psi}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{{\mathcal{I}}}(U,V).

But the directed set
\Psi
is precisely the directed set of pairs
(U,E)
where
\varphi\colon U\rightarrow L^{m}
is an analytic chart around
x
and
E\subset V
a BH-subspaces.
For such
{\mathcal{I}}=\big(\varphi\colon U\rightarrow L^{m},{\underline{r}},E\big)
with
(U,{\mathcal{I}})\in\Psi
, we moreover have
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{\mathcal{I}}(U,V)=C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
.
∎

Proposition 1.6.3
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold,
V
be a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space, and
x\in X
.

(cf.
[
30
, Satz 2.3.1]
)

The locally convex
K
-vector space
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is Hausdorff and barrelled.

(cf.
[
30
, Satz 2.3.1]
)

If
V
is of LB-type, then
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is an LB-space.

(cf.
[
30
, Satz 2.3.2]
)

If
V
is of compact type, then
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is of compact type and there is a natural topological isomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V
.

If
E
is a
K
-Banach space, then every analytic chart
\varphi
centred at
x
induces a topological isomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,E)\cong{\mathcal{A}}(L^{m},E)
where
m
is the dimension of
X
at
x
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\colon U\rightarrow B^{m}_{r}(0)
be an analytic chart centred at
x
, for some
r\vcentcolon=(r,\ldots,r)\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}^{m}
.
Let
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{L}\rvert}
with
0<\varepsilon<1
.
Then the analytic charts
U_{n}\vcentcolon=\varphi^{-1}\big(B^{m}_{\varepsilon^{n}r}(0)\big)\rightarrow B^{m}_{\varepsilon^{n}r}(0)
form a neighbourhood basis of
x
, and in view of
Lemma

1.6.2
(ii) we have

(1.23)

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\cong\varinjlim_{(n,E)}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{n},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})

where inductive limit is taken over pairs of
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and BH-subspaces
E
of
V
.
As the
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{n},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
are
K
-Banach spaces,
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is barrelled by
[
67
, Expl. 3) after Cor. 6.16]
.
Moreover, we have continuous injections

C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{n},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\cong{\mathcal{A}}_{\varepsilon^{n}r}(L^{m},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\longrightarrow\prod_{{\underline{i}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}_{0}}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}\longrightarrow\prod_{{\underline{i}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}_{0}}V

by mapping a power series to the tuple of its coefficients.
Taking the inductive limit over these, we obtain a continuous injection
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\hookrightarrow\prod_{{\underline{i}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}_{0}}V
.
It follows that
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is Hausdorff.

If
V
is of LB-type, write
V=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}
for an increasing sequence of BH-subspaces
(V_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
. Then the set of pairs
\{(n,V_{n})\mid n\in{\mathbb{N}}\}
is cofinal in the directed set of (
1.23
).
Hence
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
even is an LB-space in this case.

Now let
V
be of compact type, say
V\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}
, for a sequence of
K
-Banach spaces
(V_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
with injective compact transition homomorphisms.
Analogous to the proof of
Proposition

1.2.15
(iii), the transition maps

C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{n},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V})\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U_{n+1},\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{V})

are compact and injective, showing that
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)
is of compact type.
The topological isomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V
now follows from
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.32 (i)]
.

For a
K
-Banach space
E
, the claim of (iv) directly follows from the definition

{\mathcal{A}}(L^{m},E)=\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\varepsilon^{n}r}(L^{m},E).

∎

Proposition 1.6.4
.

Let
\varphi\colon X\rightarrow Y
be a locally
L
-analytic map between locally
L
-analytic manifolds, and let
V
be a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
For
x\in X
, the map
\varphi
induces a continuous homomorphism

\varphi^{\ast}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{\varphi(x)}(Y,V)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,V)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f\circ\varphi.

Proof.

Let
U\subset Y
be an open neighbourhood of
\varphi(x)
.
Then
\varphi^{-1}(U)
is an open neighbourhood of
x
, and the locally
L
-analytic map
\varphi^{-1}(U)\rightarrow U
induces a continuous homomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(\varphi^{-1}(U),V)
by
Proposition

1.2.14
(ii).
Via the universal property of the inductive limit, these induce the desired
\varphi^{\ast}
.
∎

Proposition 1.6.5
.

Let
X
be a locally
L
-analytic manifold and
x\in X
.
With respect to pointwise multiplication
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)
is a local
K
-algebra with maximal ideal

{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ker}}(\mathrm{ev}_{x})\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K).

Here
\mathrm{ev}_{x}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)\rightarrow K
denotes the continuous evaluation homomorphism induced by
\mathrm{ev}_{x}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(U,K)\rightarrow K
, for all open neighbourhoods
U\subset X
of
x
.

Let
m
be the dimension of
X
at
x
.
The choice of an analytic chart centred at
x
yields a topological isomorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)\cong{\mathcal{A}}(L^{m},K)
of
K
-algebras with the ring of convergent power series.

For all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}^{n}\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)
is a closed subspace of finite codimension.

Proof.

Clearly,
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)
is a
K
-algebra and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
a maximal ideal.
Via the usual inverse function theorem for power series, one shows that every
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)\setminus{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
has a multiplicative inverse, i.e. that
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{x}(X,K)
is local.

For (ii), note that the topological isomorphism in
Proposition

1.6.3
(iv) is an isomorphism of
K
-algebras.

To show that
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}^{n}
is a closed subspace of finite codimension, we may use (ii) to work with
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\mathrm{ev}_{0})^{n}\subset{\mathcal{A}}(L^{m},K)
instead.
There we have the strict epimorphism

{\mathcal{A}}_{r}(L^{m},K)\longrightarrow K^{\binom{m+n-1}{n-1}}\,,\quad\sum_{{\underline{i}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{m}_{0}}a_{{\underline{i}}}\,X^{i_{1}}\cdots X^{i_{m}}\longmapsto(a_{{\underline{i}}})_{{\lvert{{\underline{i}}}\rvert}\leq n-1},

for every
r>0
.
These induce a strict epimorphism
{\mathcal{A}}(L^{m},K)\rightarrow K^{\binom{m+n-1}{n-1}}
whose kernel precisely is
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\mathrm{ev}_{0})^{n}
.
This shows the claim of (iii).
∎

Lemma 1.6.6
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group with identity element
e
.
The multiplication
m\colon G\times G\rightarrow G
induces a continuous homomorphism of
K
-algebras

(1.24)

\Delta\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)

which is compatible with

C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K)\overset{m^{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H\times H,K)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K),

for every compact open subgroup
H\subset G
.
Moreover, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have

\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n})\subset\sum_{i=0}^{n}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{i}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n-i}.

Proof.

Let
(H_{n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be a family of compact open subgroups of
G
such that the restriction homomorphisms
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n+1},K)
are compact.
Then the inductive limit of the corresponding homomorphisms
m^{\ast}
yields a continuous homomorphism

C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K)\overset{m^{\ast}}{\longrightarrow}\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n}\times H_{n},K)

via
Lemma

1.6.2
(ii).
Moreover,

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n}\times H_{n},K)\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\big(C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K)\big)\cong C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)

by
[
67
, Expl. after Prop. 17.10]
and
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.32 (i)]
.
The resulting continuous map (
1.24
) is a homomorphism of
K
-algebras.
Now consider the continuous homomorphism

\mathrm{ev}_{e}\otimes\mathrm{ev}_{e}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\otimes_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow K\,,\quad f\otimes f^{\prime}\longmapsto f(e)f^{\prime}(e).

and the induced
\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\rightarrow K
.
We claim that

(1.25)

\big(\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}\big)\circ\Delta=\mathrm{ev}_{e}.

Indeed, it suffices to consider an compact open subgroup
H\subset G
and show the statement for

C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K)\overset{\Delta}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}}K.

Note that by density and metrizability of the completed tensor product, we can express every element of
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K)
as a convergent sum
\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}\otimes f^{\prime}_{n}
, for some
f_{n},f^{\prime}_{n}\in C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K)
.
Consequently, for
f\in C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H,K)
, we may write
\Delta(f)=\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}\otimes f^{\prime}_{n}
, so that we have
f(gg^{\prime})=\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}(g)f^{\prime}_{n}(g^{\prime})
, for all
g,g^{\prime}\in H
.
We compute that

\displaystyle\big((\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e})\circ\Delta\big)(f)=(\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e})\bigg(\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}\otimes f^{\prime}_{n}\bigg)=\sum_{n\geq 0}f_{n}(e)f^{\prime}_{n}(e)=f(e)={\mathrm{ev}}_{e}(f).

It follows from (
1.25
) that
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e})\subset{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}\big)
.
We want to show that this latter subspace of
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
equals
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}+{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
.
As the short strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow K\longrightarrow 0

consists of locally convex
K
-vector spaces of compact type (see
Proposition

A.4
), its completed tensor product with
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
remains strictly exact by
[
13
, Cor. 2.2]
:

0\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\xrightarrow{{\mathrm{id}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}{\mathrm{ev}}_{e}}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow 0.

Similarly, the map
{\mathrm{id}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}{\mathrm{ev}}_{e}
restricts to a strict epimorphism
{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\rightarrow{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}
.
Since
\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}=\mathrm{ev}_{e}\circ({\mathrm{id}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}{\mathrm{ev}}_{e})
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e})\subset{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(\mathrm{ev}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}\mathrm{ev}_{e}\big)=\big({\mathrm{id}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}{\mathrm{ev}}_{e}\big)^{-1}({\mathfrak{m}}_{e})

\displaystyle={\mathrm{Ker}}\big({\mathrm{id}}{\,\widehat{\otimes}\,}{\mathrm{ev}}_{e}\big)+{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)

\displaystyle=C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}+{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K).

The claim for
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n})
, with
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, now follows because
\Delta
is a
K
-algebra homomorphism.

∎

For a locally
L
-analytic Lie group
G
, we now want to study the strong dual of these spaces of locally analytic germs supported at
e
.
We continue to let
e
denote the identity element of
G
, and we write
D_{e}(G)=D_{e}(G,K)\vcentcolon=C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)^{\prime}_{b}
.

Remark 1.6.7
.

The
K
-algebra
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
constitutes an example of a CT-Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebra as considered by Lyubinin in
[
51
, Ch. 3.1.2]
and
[
52
, Ch. 3.2]
.

Indeed, let
H_{n}\subset G
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, be a family of compact open subgroups such that the restriction homomorphisms
r_{n}\colon C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n+1},K)
are compact.
Then each
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(H_{n},K)
is a Banach Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebra (with comultiplication
\Delta
, counit
{\mathrm{ev}}_{e}
, and antipode
{\mathrm{inv}}^{\ast}
), and the transition homomorphisms
r_{n}
are homomorphisms of Banach Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebras.

Moreover, the dual
D_{e}(G)
is an NF-Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebra
[
52
, Prop. 3.13]
.
∎

Based on the hyperalgebra
4
4

4

Often this object is called the “distribution algebra”, cf.
[
41
, I. Ch.7]
. But to avoid confusion we prefer the name “hyperalgebra” here.
classically associated with algebraic groups, we make the following definition.

Definition and Proposition 1.6.8
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group.
We define

\displaystyle{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{n}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\left\{\mu\in D_{e}(G,K)\,\middle|\,\mu({\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n+1})=0\right\}\quad\text{, for $n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}$,}

\displaystyle{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\bigcup_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{n},

and call
{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)
the
hyperalgebra
of
G
.

Then
{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{n}
is a finite-dimensional closed subspace of
D(G,K)
via the strict epimorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
.
Moreover,
{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)
is a
K
-subalgebra of
D(G,K)
with

{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{n}\ast{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{m}\subset{\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)_{n+m}\quad\text{, for all $n,m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}$.}

When the coefficient field
K
is understood implicitly, we write
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)\vcentcolon={\mathrm{hy}}(G,K)
.

Proof.

We may suppose that
G
is compact, and consider the transpose
D_{e}(G)\rightarrow D(G)
of the epimorphism
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
.
As
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)
and
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
are reflexive locally convex
K
-vector spaces, we can apply
[
11
, IV. §4.2 Cor. 1]
to conclude that
D_{e}(G)\rightarrow D(G)
is a strict injective homomorphism. We therefore may view
D_{e}(G)\subset D(G)
as a closed subspace.
We write
\iota\colon{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n+1}\hookrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
for the closed embedding of
Proposition

1.6.5
(iii).
By
[
11
, IV. §4.1 Prop. 2]
, we have

D_{e}(G)\supset{\mathrm{hy}}(G)_{n}={\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})\cong{\mathrm{Coker}}(\iota)^{\prime}

which in particular is a finite-dimensional subspace.
Thus we have realised
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)_{n}
as a closed finite-dimensional subspace of
D(G)
.

Now let
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)_{n}
and
\nu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)_{m}
.
As
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
factors over
C^{\mathrm{la}}(H,K)
, for any compact open subgroup
H\subset G
, we may assume that
G
is compact.
Then the distribution
\mu\ast\nu\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\rightarrow K
factors as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\nu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu\otimes\nu}

We know from
Lemma

1.6.6
that
\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n+m+1})\subset\sum_{i=0}^{n+m+1}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{i}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n+m+1-i}
.
Therefore we conclude that
(\mu\otimes\nu)\big(\Delta({\mathfrak{m}}_{e}^{n+m+1})\big)=0
which shows
\mu\ast\nu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)_{n+m}
.
∎

Example 1.6.9
.

Assume that
K
is a finite field extension of
L
, and let
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
be a smooth algebraic group over
L
.
In Remark
1.7.8
(ii) we will endow the group of
L
-valued points
{\mathrm{\bf G}}(L)
with the structure of a locally
L
-analytic Lie group.
Furthermore, we will see in
Corollary

1.7.10
that
{\mathrm{hy}}\big({\mathrm{\bf G}}(L)\big)
canonically agrees with
{\rm Dist}({\mathrm{\bf G}}){\,\otimes_{L}\,}K
.
Here
{\rm Dist}({\mathrm{\bf G}})
denotes the hyperalgebra (algebraic distribution algebra) as treated in
[
41
, I. Ch.7]
.
In particular if
{\rm char}(L)=0
, this is an isomorphism
{\mathrm{hy}}\big({\mathrm{\bf G}}(L)\big)\cong U({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{L}K
where
{\mathfrak{g}}
is the Lie algebra of
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
and
U({\mathfrak{g}})
its universal enveloping algebra
[
41
, I. §7.10]
.

Lemma 1.6.10
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group.
Then the topological automorphism (
1.11
) preserves
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
, i.e. it induces an automorphism

{\mathrm{hy}}(G)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{hy}}(G)\,,\quad\mu\longmapsto\dot{\mu}.

Proof.

This follows from the observations that, for every open neighbourhood
U\subset G
of
e
,
{\mathrm{inv}}(U)
is an open neighbourhood of
e
as well, and that the induced topological automorphism

{\mathrm{inv}}^{\sharp}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f\circ{\mathrm{inv}},

satisfies
{\mathrm{inv}}^{\sharp}({\mathfrak{m}}_{e})={\mathfrak{m}}_{e}
.
∎

Remark 1.6.11
.

In
[
52
, Ch. 1.2.1]
the notion of a finite dual of a Banach Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebra is defined.
With the obvious analogous definition for a CT-Hopf
\widehat{\otimes}
-algebra,
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
is the finite dual of
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
.

Proposition 1.6.12
.

Let
G
be a locally
L
-analytic Lie group, and
V\neq\{0\}
a Hausdorff locally convex
K
-vector space.
The pairing (
1.15
) induces a natural, separately continuous, non-degenerate
K
-bilinear pairing

(1.26)

D_{e}(G)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,V)\longrightarrow V\,,\quad(\mu,f)\longmapsto\mu(f)\vcentcolon=\mu(\tilde{f}),

where
\tilde{f}\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)
denotes some lift of
f
.
Restricted to
C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\subset C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,V)
, for a compact open chart
U
around
e
and a BH-subspace
E\subset V
, this pairing is given by

(1.27)

D_{e}(G)\times C^{\mathrm{rig}}(U,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\longrightarrow D_{e}(G)\times C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}\longrightarrow\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}.

Here the last map is the completed tensor product of the duality pairing between
C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
and
D_{e}(G)
with
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
.

Proof.

We may assume that
G
is compact.
To see that (
1.26
) is well defined, consider
\tilde{f}\in{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,V)\big)
.
Let
E\subset V
be a BH-subspace such that
\tilde{f}\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
.
Moreover, denote by
\tau\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)
and
\tau_{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})\rightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E})
the strict epimorphisms from
Lemma

1.6.2
(i).
Then tensoring the short strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ker}}(\tau)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}_{e}(G,K)\longrightarrow 0

with
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{E}
yields by the left exactness of the completed tensor product on locally convex
K
-vector spaces
[
13
, Lemma 2.1 (ii)]
the following diagram with exact rows

\hbox to411.22pt{\vbox to54.62pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 205.60722pt\lower-27.3095pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {

{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope {

{{{{{}}{
{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{}{{{{{}}{
