\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\big(\max(0,k_{0}),\ldots,\max(0,k_{d})\big).

We will need the following result about the Čech complex associated to the covering (
2.14
)
8
8

8

The statement of
Theorem

2.4.3
is found in the proof of
[
56
, Lemma 1.3.1]
. However the justification given there contains some flaws.
.

Theorem 2.4.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
, and
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
.
For the Čech complex
C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
associated with the covering (
2.14
) which computes the coherent cohomology
H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}})
, the differentials

d^{q}\colon C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})

are strict continuous homomorphisms when each
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})\,(={\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}}(U_{I}))\subset{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
is endowed with the topology coming from the Banach topology of
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
, for
I\subset\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}
.

Let
{\mathrm{\bf T}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf G}}
denote the split maximal torus of diagonal matrices.
For the group of its characters, we have the usual identification
X({\mathrm{\bf T}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+1}
by choosing the characters

\epsilon_{i}\colon{\mathrm{\bf T}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\,,\quad{\rm diag}(t_{0},\ldots,t_{d})\longmapsto t_{i}\quad\text{, for $i=0,\ldots,d$,}

as a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-basis.
Recall that for any
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-module
V
in the sense of
[
41
, I. §2.7]
, we have a decomposition into weight spaces
[
41
, I. §2.11]

V\cong\bigoplus_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}V_{\underline{\lambda}}\quad\text{, for $V_{\underline{\lambda}}\vcentcolon=\big\{v\in V\,\big|\,\text{$\forall$ $K$-algebras $R$, $\forall t\in{\mathrm{\bf T}}(R)$}:t.(v\otimes 1)=v\otimes{\underline{\lambda}}(t)\big\}$.}

In particular this is a decomposition into simultaneous eigenspaces with respect to the induced action of
T\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf T}}(K)
on
V
.
We say that
v\in V
is
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-homogeneous
if
v\in V_{\underline{\lambda}}
, for some
{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
.

Note that the open affine subsets
U_{i}\subset{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
are stabilized under the action of
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
, for all
i=0,\ldots,d
.
Therefore, the
K
-vector space of sections
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})
obtains the structure of a
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-module which decomposes into weight spaces

(2.17)

{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})=\bigoplus_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})_{\underline{\lambda}}.

For
x\in{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
, we let
{\mathcal{E}}(x)\vcentcolon={\mathcal{E}}_{x}/{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}{\mathcal{E}}_{x}
denote the fibre of
{\mathcal{E}}
at
x
where
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x}
is the maximal ideal of the local ring
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},x}
.
The
K
-vector space
{\mathcal{E}}(x)
is canonically isomorphic to the fibre at
x
of the geometric vector bundle associated with
{\mathcal{E}}
.

Note that the
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-action on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
has the fixed points
x_{i}\vcentcolon=[0{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}0{\,:\,}1{\,:\,}0{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}0]\in U_{i}
, for
i=0,\ldots,d
.
We therefore obtain the structure of a
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-module on
{\mathcal{E}}_{x_{i}}
.
When considering the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}
of
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
as a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant sheaf in the usual way, the maximal ideal
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x_{i}}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{x_{i}}
is a
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-submodule for the same reason.
It follows from

(2.18)

t.(se)=(t.s)(t.e)\quad\text{, for all $t\in{\mathrm{\bf T}}(R)$, $s\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{i}){\,\otimes_{K}\,}R$, and $e\in{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i}){\,\otimes_{K}\,}R$,}

that

t.(se)=(t.s)(t.e)\quad\text{, for all $t\in{\mathrm{\bf T}}(R)$, $s\in{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},x_{i}}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}R$, and $e\in{\mathcal{E}}_{x_{i}}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}R$,}

for all
K
-algebras
R
.
Hence
{\mathfrak{m}}_{x_{i}}{\mathcal{E}}_{x_{i}}\subset{\mathcal{E}}_{x_{i}}
is a
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-submodule as well, and we obtain the structure of a
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-module on the quotient
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
.

Lemma 2.4.4

(cf.
[
44
, Lemma 4.6]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
and
i\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
. For all open subsets
U\subset U_{i}
, there are
T
-equivariant isomorphisms of
{\mathcal{O}}(U)
-modules

{\mathcal{E}}(U)\cong{\mathcal{O}}(U){\,\otimes_{K}\,}{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i}),

which are compatible with respect to the restriction homomorphisms.

Proof.

By
[
41
, I.5.16]
and
[
41
, II.1.10]
, the
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
admits a trivialisation on the open affine subset
U_{i}
.
In particular, we can find sections in
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})
which globally generate
{\mathcal{E}}{\!\,\mid_{U_{i}}}
.
We may take a
K
-basis
(e_{j})_{j\in J}
, of
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})
to do so.
For
U\subset U_{i}
open, we then define the homomorphism

\varphi_{U}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(U){\,\otimes_{K}\,}{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})\,,\quad\sum_{j\in J}s_{j}e_{j}{\!\,\mid_{U}}\longmapsto\sum_{j\in J}s_{j}\otimes e_{j}(x_{i})\quad\text{, for $s_{j}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)$,}

of
{\mathcal{O}}(U)
-modules.
Note that
\varphi_{U}
is independent of the choice of the
K
-basis
(e_{j})_{j\in J}
.
An inverse to
\varphi_{U}
is given by

{\mathcal{O}}(U){\,\otimes_{K}\,}{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U)\,,\quad s\otimes\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}e_{j}(x_{i})\longmapsto\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}se_{j}{\!\,\mid_{U}}\quad\text{, for $s\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)$, $a_{j}\in K$.}

To show that
\varphi_{U}
is
T
-equivariant, let
t\in T
,
k\in J
, and
t.e_{k}=\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}e_{j}
in
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})
, for some
a_{j}\in K
.
It follows from (
2.18
) that, for all
s\in{\mathcal{O}}(U)
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{U}\big(t.(se_{k}{\!\,\mid_{U}})\big)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{U}\bigg(\sum_{j\in J}(t.s)a_{j}e_{j}{\!\,\mid_{U}}\bigg)=t.s\otimes\sum_{j\in J}a_{j}e_{j}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=t.\big(s\otimes e_{k}(x_{i})\big)=t.\varphi_{U}\big(se_{k}{\!\,\mid_{U}}\big).

The compatibility of the
\varphi_{U}
with the restriction homomorphisms is immediate.
∎

It will be helpful to fix certain norms on
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})
, for non-empty
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
, which realise its locally convex topology.
Recall that

{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})=\Bigg\{\sum_{{\underline{k}}\in\Lambda_{I}}a_{\underline{k}}\,X^{\underline{k}}\Bigg|{\lvert{a_{\underline{k}}}\rvert}\bigg(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{{\lvert{\max(0,{\underline{k}})}\rvert}}\rightarrow 0\text{ as ${\lvert{{\underline{k}}}\rvert}\rightarrow\infty$}\Bigg\},

with norm given by (
2.16
).
We now choose some
i\in I
, and let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
be a
K
-basis of weight vectors of weights
{\underline{\lambda}}_{1},\ldots,{\underline{\lambda}}_{n}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
.
We endow
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
with the norm prescribed by this basis, i.e.

\bigg\lvert\sum_{l=1}^{n}a_{l}\,v_{l}\bigg\rvert\vcentcolon=\max_{l=1}^{n}{\lvert{a_{l}}\rvert}\quad\text{, for $a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in K$.}

We continue to denote the elements of
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})
corresponding to
1\otimes v_{l}
under the isomorphism of
Lemma

2.4.4
by
v_{l}
.
Consequently, we have an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{i})
-modules

{\mathcal{O}}(U_{i})^{\oplus n}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}(U_{i})\,,\quad(f_{1},\ldots,f_{n})\longmapsto\sum_{l=1}^{n}f_{l}\,v_{l}.

This induces an isomorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
-modules
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})\cong{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})^{\oplus n}
which endows the former with the norm

(2.19)

\bigg\lvert\sum_{l=1}^{n}f_{l}\,v_{l}\bigg\lvert_{\varepsilon}\vcentcolon=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}l=1,\ldots,n\\
{\underline{k}}\in\Lambda_{I}\end{subarray}}{\lvert{a_{l,{\underline{k}}}}\rvert}\bigg(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{{\lvert{\max(0,{\underline{k}})}\rvert}}\quad\text{, for $a_{l,{\underline{k}}}\in K$ with $f_{l}=\sum_{{\underline{k}}\in\Lambda_{I}}a_{l,{\underline{k}}}\,X^{\underline{k}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})$.}

We fix this norm on
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
and on its subspace
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})\,(={\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}}(U_{I}))
, and omit the
\varepsilon
from the index.
Note that for a different choice of
i\in I
or the basis of weight vectors of
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
, the above construction yields an equivalent norm on
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
by
[
7
, 3.7.3 Prop. 3]
.

As the main step towards proving
Theorem

2.4.3
, we want to show that, for “large enough” weights
{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
, the weight spaces
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\lambda}}
change in a uniform way when one varies the “extreme” entries of
{\underline{\lambda}}
.
To this end we define, for
m\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
,

{\Delta}_{m}\vcentcolon=\left\{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})\cong{\mathbb{Z}}^{d+1}\,\middle|\,\forall j=0,\ldots,d:{\lvert{\lambda_{j}}\rvert}\leq m\right\}.

Because the
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-modules
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
are finite-dimensional, we find some
M\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that the weights of all
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{0}),\ldots,{\mathcal{E}}(x_{d})
are concentrated in
{\Delta}_{M}
, i.e. for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
and all
{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
with
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})_{\underline{\lambda}}\neq\{0\}
, we have
{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{M}
.
We moreover set
N\vcentcolon=(2d+1)M+d
.

Proposition 2.4.5
.

For every
{\underline{\mu}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
, there exist
{\underline{\nu}}\in{\Delta}_{N}
and
C\in\varepsilon^{{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}
such that, for all non-empty
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
, there is an isomorphism

\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\nu}}}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\mu}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\nu}}

of
K
-vector spaces satisfying
{\lvert{\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\nu}}}(v)}\rvert}=C\,{\lvert{v}\rvert}
, for all
v\in{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})_{\underline{\mu}}
, and such that these isomorphisms are compatible with the restriction homomorphisms, i.e. for all
I\subset J\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
, the following diagram commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})_{\underline{\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\nu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})_{\underline{\nu}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\nu}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{J}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\nu}}}}

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
\lVert{\underline{\mu}}\rVert\vcentcolon=\sum_{j=0}^{d}{\lvert{\mu_{j}}\rvert}
.
Note that for
{\underline{\mu}}\in{\Delta}_{N}
, we may take
{\underline{\nu}}\vcentcolon={\underline{\mu}}
,
C\vcentcolon=1
, and the identity homomorphisms to obtain the assertion of the proposition.
This also deals with the base case
\lVert{\underline{\mu}}\rVert=0
.

Hence we now suppose that
{\underline{\mu}}\notin{\Delta}_{N}
.
We let
u,v\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
such that
\mu_{u}
is maximal among the entries
\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{d}
and the entry
\mu_{v}
is minimal.
We set
{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}\vcentcolon={\underline{\mu}}-\epsilon_{u}+\epsilon_{v}
.

For fixed
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
with
i\in I
, let
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\in{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
be a
K
-basis of weight vectors of weights
{\underline{\lambda}}_{1},\ldots,{\underline{\lambda}}_{n}
.
We then have the following
K
-basis for the weight space

{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\mu}}=\bigoplus_{l\in L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}}K\cdot X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}

where
L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}\vcentcolon=\big\{l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}\,\big|\,X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})\big\}
.

Lemma 2.4.6
.

For
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we have

X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I}).

If this is the case, for some
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
, we moreover have
\mu_{u}>M+1
and
\mu_{v}<-(M+1)
so that in particular
u\neq v
.

Proof.

We first note that
X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
if and only if
\sum_{j=0}^{d}\mu_{j}-\lambda_{l,j}=0
and, for all
j\in\{0,\ldots,d\}\setminus I
, we have
\mu_{j}-\lambda_{l,j}\geq 0
.
For
X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}
the analogous statement holds.
To show the claimed equivalence it thus suffices to focus on the exponents of
X_{u}
and
X_{v}
.

Because
{\underline{\mu}}\notin{\Delta}_{N}
, there exists
j\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
such that
{\lvert{\mu_{j}}\rvert}>N
.
We distinguish the two cases that
\mu_{j}>0
and that
\mu_{j}<0
.
If
\mu_{j}>0
, then
\mu_{u}>N\geq M+1
by the maximality of
\mu_{u}
.
Using
{\lvert{\lambda_{l,u}}\rvert}\leq M
we find that

\mu_{u}-1-\lambda_{l,u}>N-1-M\geq 0

and also
\mu_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}\geq 0
, so that the exponent of
X_{u}
is not an obstacle in this case.
Concerning the exponent of
X_{v}
, since
({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}\geq\mu_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}
, we find that if
X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
, then
X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
.
Conversely suppose that
X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
so that in particular
\sum_{j=0}^{d}\big(({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{j}-\lambda_{l,j}\big)=0
and
({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}\geq 0
.
We compute

\displaystyle\bigg\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}\mu_{j}\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}(\mu_{j}-\lambda_{l,j})+\sum_{j=0}^{d}\lambda_{l,j}\bigg\rvert=\bigg\lvert\underset{=0}{\underbrace{\sum_{j=0}^{d}\big(({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{j}-\lambda_{l,j}\big)}}+\sum_{j=0}^{d}\lambda_{l,j}\bigg\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}\lambda_{l,j}\bigg\rvert\leq\sum_{j=0}^{d}{\lvert{\lambda_{l,j}}\rvert}\leq(d+1)M.

Then

(d+1)M\geq\bigg\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}\mu_{j}\bigg\rvert=\bigg\lvert\mu_{u}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq u\end{subarray}}^{d}\mu_{j}\bigg\rvert

together with
\mu_{u}>N=(2d+1)M+d
implies that
\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq u\end{subarray}}^{d}\mu_{j}<-d(M+1)
.
Hence there exists
j^{\prime}\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
,
j^{\prime}\neq u
, such that
\mu_{j^{\prime}}<-(M+1)
.
By the minimality of
\mu_{v}
, it follows that
\mu_{v}<-(M+1)
.
But using
{\lvert{\lambda_{l,v}}\rvert}\leq M
we find that

\displaystyle({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}

\displaystyle=\mu_{v}+1-\lambda_{l,v}<-(M+1)+1+M=0.

Therefore
X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
can only occur if
v\in I
.
We conclude that
X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
as well which finishes the proof in this case.

Now we consider the case that
\mu_{j}<0
.
Here we find that
\mu_{v}<-N\leq-(M+1)
by the minimality of
\mu_{v}
.
Like before, we have

(d+1)M\geq\bigg\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}\mu_{j}\bigg\rvert=\bigg\lvert\mu_{v}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq v\end{subarray}}^{d}\mu_{j}\bigg\rvert.

From this and
\mu_{v}<-N
we conclude that there exists
j^{\prime}\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
,
j^{\prime}\neq v
, such that
\mu_{j^{\prime}}>M+1
.
As
\mu_{u}
is maximal, it follows that
\mu_{u}>M+1
as well.
Therefore

\displaystyle({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}

\displaystyle=\mu_{u}-1-\lambda_{l,u}>(M+1)-1-M=0,

and moreover
\mu_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}\geq 0
.
This shows that the exponent of
X_{u}
is not an obstacle in this case.
For the exponent of
X_{v}
, we compute

\displaystyle({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}

\displaystyle=\mu_{v}+1-\lambda_{l,v}<-N+1+M<0,

and also
\mu_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}<0
.
Therefore either of
X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
or
X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
implies that
v\in I
.
This shows the assertion in the case
\mu_{j}<0
.
∎

Using the above lemma, we see that

{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}=\bigoplus_{l\in L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}}K\cdot X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l},

and we define the following isomorphism of
K
-vector spaces

\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{{\underline{\mu}}}\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}\,,\quad X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\longmapsto X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}.

For
l\in L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}
, it follows from
\mu_{u}>M+1
,
\mu_{v}<-(M+1)
, and
{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}\in{\Delta}_{M}
that

\displaystyle({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}

\displaystyle\geq 0,\hskip-56.9055pt

\displaystyle\mu_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

\displaystyle({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}

\displaystyle\leq 0,\hskip-56.9055pt

\displaystyle\mu_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}

\displaystyle\leq 0.

Therefore

\displaystyle\max\big(0,({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}\big)

\displaystyle=\mu_{u}-1-\lambda_{l,u}

\displaystyle=\max\big(0,\mu_{u}-\lambda_{l,u}\big)-1,

\displaystyle\max\big(0,({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}\big)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle=\max\big(0,\mu_{v}-\lambda_{l,v}\big),

and we conclude that

\displaystyle\big\lvert X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\big\rvert

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{\sum_{j=0}^{d}\max\big(0,({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})_{j}-\lambda_{l,j}\big)}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\bigg)^{\big(\sum_{j=0}^{d}\max(0,\mu_{j}-\lambda_{l,j})\big)-1}=\varepsilon\,\big\lvert X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\big\rvert.

Hence we have
{\lvert{\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}(v)}\rvert}=\varepsilon\,{\lvert{v}\rvert}
, for all
v\in{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\mu}}
.

Next we verify that the above family of isomorphisms
\varphi^{I}\vcentcolon=\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}
is compatible with the restriction maps.
For this consider
I\subset J\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
with
i\in I
and
j\in J
.
Let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})=\bigoplus_{l=1}^{n}{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})\,v_{l}\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})=\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n}{\mathcal{O}}(U_{J})\,w_{k},

for a
K
-basis consisting of weight vectors
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}
of
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{i})
of weights
{\underline{\lambda}}_{1},\ldots,{\underline{\lambda}}_{n}
, and a
K
-basis consisting of weight vectors
w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}
of
{\mathcal{E}}(x_{j})
of weights
{\underline{\kappa}}_{1},\ldots,{\underline{\kappa}}_{n}
which yield the isomorphisms
\varphi^{I}
and
\varphi^{J}
respectively.
Let
{\rm res}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})
denote the restriction map which is
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
-linear and injective.
Furthermore, there are
a_{l,k}\in K
such that

{\rm res}(v_{l})=\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{l,k}\,X^{{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}-{\underline{\kappa}}_{k}}\,w_{k}\quad\text{ , for all $l=1,\ldots,n$,}

because
{\rm res}(v_{l})\in{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})
is of weight
{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}
.
For
l\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
such that
X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\in{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I})
we now compute

\displaystyle{\rm res}\Big(\varphi^{I}\big(X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle={\rm res}\Big(X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\Big)=X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,{\rm res}(v_{l})

\displaystyle=X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{l,k}\,X^{{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}-{\underline{\kappa}}_{k}}\,w_{k}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{l,k}\,X^{({\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v})-{\underline{\kappa}}_{k}}\,w_{k}

\displaystyle=\varphi^{J}\bigg(\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{l,k}\,X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\kappa}}_{k}}\,w_{k}\bigg)=\varphi^{J}\bigg(X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}a_{l,k}\,X^{{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}-{\underline{\kappa}}_{k}}\,w_{k}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\varphi^{J}\big(X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,{\rm res}(v_{l})\big)=\varphi^{J}\Big({\rm res}\big(X^{{\underline{\mu}}-{\underline{\lambda}}_{l}}\,v_{l}\big)\Big)

which shows the compatibility for
I\subset J
.

Finally, we want to apply the induction hypothesis.
In the case that, for all
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
with
I\neq\emptyset
, the set
L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}
is empty, we find that
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\mu}}=\{0\}
, for all such
I
.
We may for example take
{\underline{\nu}}\vcentcolon=(M+1,\ldots,M+1)\in{\Delta}_{N}
, and see that, for all such
I
and all
l=1,\ldots,n
, we have
{\underline{\nu}}-{\underline{\mu}}_{l}\notin\Lambda_{I}
.
It follows that
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\nu}}=\{0\}
, and we obtain the sought isomorphisms trivially.

On the other hand, if there is some
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
with
I\neq\emptyset
for which
L_{{\underline{\mu}},I}
is non-empty, the second assertion of
Lemma

2.4.6
implies that

\displaystyle\lVert{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}\rVert

\displaystyle={\lvert{\mu_{u}-1}\rvert}+{\lvert{\mu_{v}+1}\rvert}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq u,v\end{subarray}}^{d}{\lvert{\mu_{j}}\rvert}=(\mu_{u}-1)-(\mu_{v}+1)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j=0\\
j\neq u,v\end{subarray}}^{d}{\lvert{\mu_{j}}\rvert}=\lVert{\underline{\mu}}\rVert-2.

Applying the induction hypothesis to
{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}
, we obtain
{\underline{\nu}}\in{\Delta}_{N}
and the family of isomorphisms
\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v},{\underline{\nu}}}
, for
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
, as specified.
Then the statement follows for
{\underline{\mu}}
by taking the compositions

\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\nu}}}\vcentcolon=\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v},{\underline{\nu}}}\circ\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\mu}},{\underline{\mu}}-\alpha_{u,v}}.

∎

Proof of
Theorem

2.4.3
.

Recall that in (
2.19
), for every non-empty
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
, we fixed a norm on
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})
.
This endows the space of
q
-th Čech cochains, for
q\geq 0
,

C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})=\bigoplus_{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{q})\in\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}^{q+1}}{\mathcal{E}}\big(U_{\{i_{0},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\big)

with a norm as well.
We also have a decomposition of
C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
into weight spaces

C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}=\bigoplus_{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{q})\in\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}^{q+1}}{\mathcal{E}}\big(U_{\{i_{0},\ldots,i_{q}\}}\big)_{\underline{\lambda}}\quad\text{, for ${\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})$,}

under the induced
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-action.
Note that if we have a weight decomposition of
f\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
, it follows from (
2.19
) that

{\lvert{f}\rvert}=\sup_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}{\lvert{f_{\lambda}}\rvert}\quad\text{, for $f=\sum_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}f_{\underline{\lambda}}$, with $f_{\underline{\lambda}}\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}$.}

Since the restriction maps
{\rm res}_{I,J}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{J})
, for
I\subset J\subset\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}
, are
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-equivariant, so is the differential

\displaystyle d^{q}\colon C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}),

\displaystyle\big(f_{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{q})}\big)_{(i_{0},\ldots,i_{q})}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\bigg(\sum_{k=0}^{q+1}(-1)^{k}{\rm res}_{I,J}\big(f_{(j_{0},\ldots,\widehat{j_{k}},\ldots,j_{q+1})}\big)\bigg)_{(j_{0},\ldots,j_{q+1})},

of the Čech complex.
Therefore we may restrict
d^{q}
to the individual weight spaces

d^{q}_{{\underline{\lambda}}}\colon C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}\quad\text{, for ${\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})$.}

Now let
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
be defined as before
Proposition

2.4.5
, and consider

d^{q}_{N}\colon\bigoplus_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{N}}C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{N}}C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}\,,\quad\big(f_{\underline{\lambda}}\big)_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{N}}\longmapsto\big(d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}}(f_{\underline{\lambda}})\big)_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{N}}.

Since this homomorphism takes values in a finite-dimensional
K
-vector space,
d^{q}_{N}
is strict by
[
11
, I.2.3. Cor.]
.
Hence there exists
R>0
such that

(2.20)

B_{R}(0)\cap{\mathrm{Im}}(d^{q}_{N})\subset d_{N}^{q}\big(B_{1}(0)\big)

where
B_{R}(0)
and
B_{1}(0)
denote the “closed” balls of radius
R
and
1
respectively.

To see that
d^{q}
itself is strict, it suffices to show that

(2.21)

B_{R}(0)\cap{\mathrm{Im}}(d^{q})\subset d^{q}\big(B_{1}(0)\big)

by scaling.
For this, let
g\in{\mathrm{Im}}(d^{q})\cap B_{R}(0)
, and let
g=\sum_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}g_{\underline{\lambda}}
be a weight decomposition with
g_{\underline{\lambda}}\in C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
As
{\lvert{g}\rvert}=\sup_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}{\lvert{g_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}
, we have
{\lvert{g_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}\leq R
, for all
{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
.
We moreover have
g_{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\mathrm{Im}}(d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}})
since
d^{q}
is
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
-equivariant.
For
{\underline{\lambda}}\in{\Delta}_{N}
, we can therefore conclude by (
2.20
) that there exists
f_{\underline{\lambda}}\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}
such that
{\lvert{f_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}\leq 1
and
d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}}(f_{\underline{\lambda}})=g_{\underline{\lambda}}
.

For
{\underline{\lambda}}\notin{\Delta}_{N}
in turn, we apply
Proposition

2.4.5
to find
{\underline{\nu}}\in{\Delta}_{N}
,
C\in\varepsilon^{{\mathbb{N}}_{0}}
, and a compatible family of isomorphisms

\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}\colon{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\nu}}\quad\text{, for all non-empty $I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}$,}

such that
\lvert\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}(v)\rvert=C\,{\lvert{v}\rvert}
, for all
v\in{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I})_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
We take the direct sum of these isomorphisms to obtain isomorphisms

\varphi^{q}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}\colon C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\nu}}\quad\text{, for all $q\geq 0$,}

such that
\lvert\varphi^{q}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}(v)\rvert=C\,{\lvert{v}\rvert}
, for all
v\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}
.
Moreover, the compatibility of the
\varphi^{I}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}
with the canonical restriction maps implies that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\nu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{q+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\nu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{q}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi^{q+1}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{q}_{{\underline{\nu}}}}

commutes.
Since
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
, we may assume that
\varepsilon={\lvert{a}\rvert}
, for some
a\in K
, after an extension of scalars by a finite extension of
K
.
Then by scaling (
2.20
) by an appropriate power of
a
and restricting to the vectors which are rational with respect to the original
K
, we see that

B_{CR}(0)\cap{\mathrm{Im}}(d^{q}_{N})\subset d^{q}_{N}\big(B_{C}(0)\big).

Because we have
\lvert\varphi^{q+1}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}(g_{\underline{\lambda}})\rvert=C\,{\lvert{g_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}\leq CR
, we find
h\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\nu}}
such that
{\lvert{h}\rvert}\leq C
and
d^{q}_{\underline{\nu}}(h)=\varphi^{q+1}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}(g_{\underline{\lambda}})
.
Then
f_{\underline{\lambda}}\vcentcolon=\big(\varphi^{q}_{{\underline{\lambda}},{\underline{\nu}}}\big)^{-1}(h)
satisfies
d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}}(f_{\underline{\lambda}})=g_{\underline{\lambda}}
and
{\lvert{f_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}=C^{-1}{\lvert{h}\rvert}\leq 1
.

In total we obtain, for every
{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})
, an element
f_{\underline{\lambda}}\in C^{q}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})_{\underline{\lambda}}
such that
d^{q}_{\underline{\lambda}}(f_{\underline{\lambda}})=g_{\underline{\lambda}}
and
{\lvert{f_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}\leq 1
.
Taking the sum

f\vcentcolon=\sum_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}f_{\underline{\lambda}}

we find that
d^{q}(f)=g
and that
{\lvert{f}\rvert}=\sup_{{\underline{\lambda}}\in X({\mathrm{\bf T}})}{\lvert{f_{\underline{\lambda}}}\rvert}\leq 1
.
This shows (
2.21
) and therefore the strictness of
d^{q}
.
∎

2.5.
Local Cohomology with respect to Tubes around Schubert Varieties

We begin by fixing some notation.
For
{r}\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}
and
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
, we set

\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)

and endow it with the subspace topology.
We define
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
,
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
, and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
analogously.

Lemma 2.5.1
.

Let
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}<1
with
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
, and
I\subset\{0,\ldots,d\}
.
Then the transition map

{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})

is a compact homomorphism between
K
-Banach spaces with dense image.

Proof.

If
I=\emptyset
, we have
U_{I,\varepsilon}=U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}}={\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
and
{\mathcal{E}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})
is a finite dimensional
K
-vector space.
Therefore, the homomorphism in question is compact by
[
67
, Lemma 16.4]
.

Now suppose that
I\neq\emptyset
.
Our method here is similar to the proof of
Lemma

2.3.3
.
For
I=\{i_{0},\ldots,i_{m}\}
, we consider the affinoid
K
-algebra

\displaystyle A_{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=K\big\langle\varepsilon T_{(j,i_{k})}\,\big|\,j=0,\ldots,d,k=0,\ldots,m\text{, with }j\neq i_{k}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\Bigg\{\sum_{{\underline{\mu}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d(m+1)}_{0}}a_{{\underline{\mu}}}\,{\underline{T}}^{{\underline{\mu}}}\in K\mkern-3.0mu\left[\mkern-2.5mu\left[{\underline{T}}\right]\mkern-2.5mu\right]\,\Bigg|\,{\lvert{a_{{\underline{\mu}}}}\rvert}\left(\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\right)^{{\lvert{{\underline{\mu}}}\rvert}}\rightarrow 0\text{ as ${\lvert{{\underline{\mu}}}\rvert}\rightarrow\infty$}\Bigg\},

cf.
[
7
, 6.1.5 Thm. 4]
.
Like in the proof of
Lemma

2.4.1
, we have
U_{I,\varepsilon}={\mathrm{Sp}}(A_{\varepsilon}/{\mathfrak{a}}A_{\varepsilon})
, for the ideal

{\mathfrak{a}}=\big(T_{(i_{j},i_{k})}T_{(i_{k},i_{j})}-1\,\big|\,j,k=0,\ldots,m\text{, with }j\neq k\big).

With similar definitions for
U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}}
, we obtain a commutative diagram of affinoid
K
-algebras

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\varepsilon}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\varepsilon}/{\mathfrak{a}}A_{\varepsilon}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}/{\mathfrak{a}}A_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}

where the vertical maps are strict epimorphisms, and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
is the transition homomorphism
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})\rightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})
.
We claim that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
is an inner homomorphism of affinoid
K
-algebras in the sense of
[
5
, Def. 2.5.1]
.
Indeed we have, for
T=T_{(j,i_{k})}\in A_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}}}{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}(\tau(T))(x)}\rvert}=\sup_{x\in U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}}}{\lvert{\tau^{\prime}(\psi(T))(x)}\rvert}\leq{\lvert{\psi(T)}\rvert}_{\sup}={\lvert{\psi(T)}\rvert}_{A_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}}=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}<\frac{1}{\varepsilon}.

It follows from
[
70
, §1 Lemma 5]
that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
is a compact homomorphism of locally convex
K
-vector spaces.

For a general
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}
, we again may apply Kiehl’s theorem
[
7
, 9.4.3 Thm. 3]
to find strict epimorphisms
{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})^{\oplus k}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})
and
{{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})^{\oplus k}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})}
which are compatible, for some
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.
Then
Lemma

A.3
(ii) and (iii) applied to

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})^{\oplus k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{O}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})^{\oplus k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}}^{\oplus k}}

show that
{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon})\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U_{I,\varepsilon^{\prime}})
is compact.

Finally it follows from
Lemma

2.4.1
that the image of the transition map is dense.
∎

Proposition 2.5.2
.

Let
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
.
Let
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
be some strictly decreasing sequence with
\varepsilon_{m}\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
,
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{m}<1
, and
\varepsilon_{m}\rightarrow\varepsilon
.
Then the
K
-Fréchet space
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is the projective limit of the
K
-Banach spaces

(2.22)

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}).

The transition homomorphisms

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\quad\text{, for $\varepsilon_{m}<\varepsilon_{n}$,}

which are induced from the inclusion
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}
are compact and have dense image so that
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is nuclear.
Moreover, the local cohomology group
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
of the algebraic variety
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
constitutes a dense subspace of
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

The analogous statements for
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
hold as well.

Proof.

We first focus on the
K
-Banach spaces associated with the “closed” neighbourhoods of
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}
. For now we fix
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
and consider the Čech complex
C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
associated with the covering (
2.12
) which computes the cohomology groups
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
.
For this Čech complex, we let

\displaystyle Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ker}}\big(C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\overset{d^{i}_{\varepsilon}}{\longrightarrow}C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\big),

\displaystyle B^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Im}}\big(C^{i-1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\overset{d^{i-1}_{\varepsilon}}{\longrightarrow}C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\big)

denote the space of
i
-th Čech cocycles respectively the space of
i
-th Čech coboundaries.
We use the analogous notation for further Čech complexes that will occur.

Recall from
Lemma

2.4.1
that the cochains
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
have the structure of a
K
-Banach space, and the cochains
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
of algebraic sections constitute a dense subspace therein.
As a first step we want to see that the induced locally convex topology on
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
,
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
, and
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
gives those spaces the structure of
K
-Banach spaces as well, and that their “algebraic” counterparts
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
,
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
, and
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
embed as dense subspaces respectively.

To this end we consider
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
with the subspace topology coming from
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
so that
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
.
We will endow all “algebraic” terms with the topologies induced from
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
, and all “analytic” terms with the ones induced from
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
.

From
Theorem

2.4.3
we know that the differential
d^{i}\colon C^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\rightarrow C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
is strict, and the differential
d^{i}_{\varepsilon}\colon C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
d^{i}
.
Therefore
[
7
, 1.1.9 Prop. 5]
implies that
Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
, and
B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
.
Moreover note that
d^{i}_{\varepsilon}
is strict by
[
7
, 1.1.9 Prop. 4]
, i.e.
B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
is a closed subspace of
C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
so that
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
is a
K
-Banach space.

We also have the short strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow B^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow 0.

The completion of this sequence yields the short strictly exact sequence
[
7
, 1.1.9 Cor. 6]

0\longrightarrow B^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})^{{\,\widehat{}}}\longrightarrow 0.

Therefore the map
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})^{\,\widehat{}}\rightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
obtained by the universal property of the completion is a topological isomorphism, and
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
.

Now recall the long exact sequence of local cohomology

(2.23)

\displaystyle\ldots\longrightarrow H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\alpha^{i-1}}H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\partial^{i-1}}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\xrightarrow{\beta^{i}}H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow\ldots.

We endow the local cohomology group
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
with the locally convex final topology with respect to
\partial^{i-1}
.
As seen in
Remark

2.1.1
this makes
\partial^{i-1}
strict and
\beta^{i}
continuous.
Furthermore, the homomorphisms
\alpha^{i}
and
\beta^{i}
are strict as well since
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
is a finite-dimensional locally convex
K
-vector space
[
59
, Cor. 3.4.25]
.
The analogous situation arises for
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}\subset{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
.

From this long strictly exact sequence, we obtain the following commutative diagram with strictly exact rows

(2.24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{i-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon}}

Because
\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon}
is a strict epimorphism, it follows that
{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})\rightarrow{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})
is a strict epimorphism
[
76
, Prop. 1.1.8]
.

From (
2.23
) we moreover obtain the commutative diagram

(2.25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{i-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{i-1}_{\varepsilon}}

with strictly exact rows.
Here we have used the exactness of (
2.23
) for the identification
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\partial^{i-1})={\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})
and
{\mathrm{Im}}(\partial^{i-1})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\beta^{i})=\vcentcolon\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
, and likewise for the “analytic” terms.
The composition
{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})\rightarrow H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\rightarrow H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
is a strict monomorphism
[
76
, Prop. 1.1.7]
.
Therefore
{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})\rightarrow{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})
is a strict monomorphism as well
[
76
, Prop. 1.1.8]
, and we conclude that
{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha^{i-1})\cong{\mathrm{Im}}(\alpha_{\varepsilon}^{i-1})
.
By taking the completion of the first row of (
2.25
) and reasoning similarly to before, we find that
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
.

From (
2.24
) we also can conclude that
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i-1})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i-1}_{\varepsilon})
by applying the appropriate version of the snake lemma
A.13
.
Since
{\mathrm{Im}}(\beta^{i})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i})
and
{\mathrm{Im}}(\beta^{i}_{\varepsilon})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon})
by the exactness of (
2.23
), we arrive at the short strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i})\longrightarrow 0.

The completion of this sequence yields the following commutative diagram with strictly exact rows
[
7
, 1.1.9 Prop. 4, Cor. 6]

(2.26)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})^{\,\widehat{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})^{\,\widehat{}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Via the snake lemma
A.13
it follows that the vertical homomorphism in the middle is a topological isomorphism, too.
Therefore
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is the completion of
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})
.

Fixing
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
and a strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}
as specified, we now consider
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{m}<\varepsilon_{n}<1
with
\varepsilon_{m},\varepsilon_{n}\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
.
Using the commutativity of

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}^{\mathrm{alg}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}

it follows from the statements about the density of the “algebraic” subspaces which we have just shown that the transition homomorphism
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
has dense image.
Analogously one argues for
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

To show that the transition homomorphisms are compact, we start at the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{n}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{n}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{n}},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{i}_{\varepsilon_{n}}}

with strictly exact rows.
We have seen in
Lemma

2.5.1
that the vertical homomorphism in the middle is compact.
Hence
Lemma

A.3
(i) implies that
Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{n}},{\mathcal{E}})
is compact.
Using
Lemma

A.3
(ii) one argues in the analogous way to conclude that
H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
are compact.
For

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{n}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

the short strictly exact sequences of locally convex
K
-vector spaces in both rows split compatibly as
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha_{\varepsilon_{m}}^{i})\cong{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{n}})
is finite-dimensional
[
59
, Cor. 3.4.27]
.
Therefore the transition homomorphism
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is compact by
Lemma

A.3
(iii).

Remark 2.5.3
.

Having seen that the transition maps
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
have dense image, we can apply
[
56
, Prop. 1.3.3]
to obtain the topological isomorphism (
2.22
) at this point.
However, we will additionally present the alternative way mentioned in
[
56
, Rmk. 1.3.6]
to do so.
To this end, one needs the following lemma.

Lemma 2.5.4

(cf.
[
56
, Lemma 1.3.7]
)
.

Consider a projective system of strictly exact sequences

0\longrightarrow V^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow V_{n}\longrightarrow V^{\prime\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow 0\quad\text{, for $n\in{\mathbb{N}}$,}

of
K
-Fréchet spaces.
If the transition maps
V^{\prime}_{n+1}\rightarrow V^{\prime}_{n}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, have dense image, then the sequence

(2.27)

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V^{\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V^{\prime\prime}_{n}\longrightarrow 0

is strictly exact, too.

Proof.

We may view
V^{\prime}_{n}
as the kernel of
V_{n}\rightarrow V^{\prime\prime}_{n}
.
Since taking the (projective) limit commutes with kernels, we see that
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V^{\prime}_{n}
is the kernel of
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V^{\prime\prime}_{n}
.

Moreover, as the transition homomorphisms
V^{\prime}_{n+1}\rightarrow V^{\prime}_{n}
have dense image, the topological Mittag-Leffler condition is fulfilled for this inverse system.
It follows that (
2.27
) is a short exact sequence of vector spaces
[
34
, 13.2.4 (i)]
.
Finally, the open mapping theorem
[
67
, Prop. 8.6]
implies that
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V^{\prime\prime}_{n}
is strict, too.
∎

The differential
d^{i}_{\varepsilon}\colon C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
is the projective limit of the differentials
d^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}}\colon C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow C^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})
.
Therefore we have
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})
and
Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})
because the (projective) limit commutes with taking kernels.
But by
Lemma

2.5.4
, there is the short strictly exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}Z^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow 0

so that we can conclude
B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}B^{i+1}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon_{m}},{\mathcal{E}})
.
In a similar way we obtain
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon),{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
.

Likewise arguing for the homomorphisms
\beta^{i}_{\varepsilon}=\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\beta^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}}
and
\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon}=\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}}
, we find that
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
and
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha_{\varepsilon}^{i})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha_{\varepsilon_{m}}^{i})
.
We now take the projective limit over the projective system

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}})\longrightarrow 0

of short strictly exact sequences to arrive at the following commutative diagram with strictly exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim\limits_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim\limits_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varprojlim\limits_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon_{m}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ker}}(\alpha^{i}_{\varepsilon})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

Since the outer vertical maps are topological isomorphisms, it follows from the snake lemma
A.13
that
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.
∎

The same reasoning shows the following statement in the extreme case
\varepsilon=0
.

Corollary 2.5.5
.

For any strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\subset{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
with
0<\varepsilon_{m}<1
and
\varepsilon_{m}\rightarrow 0
, the
K
-Fréchet space
H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is the projective limit of the
K
-Banach spaces

H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow 0}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}).

with compact transition maps which have dense image.
Moreover, the local cohomology group
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
of the algebraic variety
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
constitutes a dense subspace of
H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

For
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
the analogous assertions hold.

There also is a result for the projective limit of the local cohomology groups
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
with respect to the “open”
\varepsilon
-neighbourhoods.

Corollary 2.5.6
.

For any strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\subset{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
with
0<\varepsilon_{m}<1
and
\varepsilon_{m}\rightarrow 0
, there is a topological isomorphism

(2.28)

H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\cong\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow 0}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})

of
K
-Fréchet spaces.
Moreover, the transition homomorphisms

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\quad\text{, for $\varepsilon_{m}<\varepsilon_{n}$,}

are compact and have dense image.

Again, the analogous statements are true for
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

For the topological isomorphism (
2.28
) one argues analogously to the last part of the proof of the preceeding proposition.
To show that the topological Mittag-Leffler condition for
Lemma

2.5.4
is fulfilled one uses the statement of
Proposition

2.5.2
about the density of
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\subset H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

To see that the transition homomorphisms are compact, note that, for
\varepsilon_{m}<\varepsilon_{n}
, the transition map factors as

(2.29)

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}).

The first homomorphism is a continuous linear map from a nuclear locally convex
K
-vector space to a
K
-Banach space and therefore is compact by
[
67
, Prop. 19.5]
.
It follows from
[
67
, Rmk. 16.7 (i)]
that the composition (
2.29
) is compact as well.

For
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
one argues analogously.
∎

Proposition 2.5.7
.

We have the following description of the local cohomology groups

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ , for $i<d-{r}$ or $i>d$,}\\
H^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{ , for $i=d-{r}$,}\\
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{ , for $i>d-{r}$.}\end{cases}

For
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
, we have

H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})=\begin{cases}0&\text{ , for $i<d-{r}$ or $i>d$,}\\
H^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{ , for $i=d-{r}$,}\\
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{ , for $i>d-{r}$,}\end{cases}

and similarly for
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
and
H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
.

Proof.

The statement for
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is shown in
[
56
, pp. 595–597]
(and the reasoning there is independent of the field
K
).
For
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
,
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
, and
H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
the assertion then follows from the density of the “algebraic” local cohomology groups. ∎

2.6.
Local Cohomology Groups with respect to Schubert Varieties as Locally Analytic Representations

We let
{\mathrm{\bf B}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf G}}={\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,K}
denote the Borel subgroup of lower triangular matrices, and
{\mathrm{\bf T}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf G}}
the maximal torus of diagonal matrices.
For
i=0,\ldots,d
, let
\epsilon_{i}\colon{\mathrm{\bf T}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
be the character defined via
\epsilon_{i}({\rm diag}(t_{0},\ldots,t_{d}))=t_{i}
, and set
\alpha_{i,j}\vcentcolon=\epsilon_{i}-\epsilon_{j}
, for
i\neq j
, and
\alpha_{i}\vcentcolon=\alpha_{i+1,i}
, for
i=0,\ldots,d-1
.
Then the roots of
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
with respect to
{\mathrm{\bf T}}
are

\Phi=\{\alpha_{i,j}\mid 0\leq i\neq j\leq d\}

and its simple roots with respect to
{\mathrm{\bf T}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf B}}
are

\Delta=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-1}\}.

Moreover, for
I\subset\Delta
, we let
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I}\subset{\mathrm{\bf G}}
denote the (lower) standard parabolic subgroup associated with
I
, i.e. the subgroup generated by
{\mathrm{\bf B}}
and the root subgroups
{\mathrm{\bf U}}_{-\alpha}
, for
\alpha\in I
.
For example, we have
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{\emptyset}={\mathrm{\bf B}}
and
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{\Delta}={\mathrm{\bf G}}
.
We write
P_{I}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I}(K)
which is a locally
K
-analytic subgroup of
G
.
We set
P_{I,0}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
which is a compact open subgroup of
P_{I}
.
Here
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}
denotes the respective standard parabolic subgroup of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,{\mathbb{Z}}}
.
Additionally, consider the canonical reduction homomorphism

p_{n}\colon G_{0}\longrightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}/{\pi}^{n}),

for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
We define

P^{n}_{I}\vcentcolon=p_{n}^{-1}\big({\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}/{\pi}^{n})\big)

which is an open compact subgroup of
G_{0}
containing
P_{I,0}
.

We now fix
{r}\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}
.
Then the maximal parabolic subgroup
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{r}\}}
stabilizes the subvariety
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}
of
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
.
Consequently
P_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{r}\}}
stabilizes
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}
under the group action of
G
on
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{rig}}
.

We claim that, for any
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
with
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}\leq\varepsilon<1
, the subgroup
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\subset G_{0}
stabilizes
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)
.
Indeed, for
[z_{0}{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}z_{d}]\in{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)
and
g=(g_{ij})\in P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
, let us write
g^{-1}z=\vcentcolon[w_{0}{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}w_{d}]
.
We thus have, for
j={r}+1,\ldots,d
,

\displaystyle{\lvert{w_{j}}\rvert}=\bigg\lvert\sum_{i=0}^{d}z_{i}g_{ij}\bigg\rvert\leq\max\Big(\max_{i=0}^{r}{\lvert{z_{i}g_{ij}}\rvert},\max_{i={r}+1}^{d}{\lvert{z_{i}g_{ij}}\rvert}\Big)\leq\max\Big(\max_{i=0}^{r}1\cdot{\lvert{{\pi}^{n}}\rvert},\max_{i={r}+1}^{d}\varepsilon\cdot 1\Big)\leq\varepsilon.

Analogously one computes that
{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}
is stabilized by
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
, for any
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}<\varepsilon<1
.

Proposition 2.6.1

(cf.
[
56
, Cor. 1.3.9]
)
.

Let
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}<\varepsilon<1
.
Then the representation

P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\times\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad(g,\ell)\longmapsto\ell(g^{-1}{\,\_\,}),

is locally analytic.
Moreover, for any strictly decreasing sequence
(\varepsilon_{m})_{m\in{\mathbb{N}}}\subset{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
with
\varepsilon_{m}\rightarrow\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{m}<1
, the canonical map

\varinjlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\longrightarrow\Big(\varprojlim_{\varepsilon_{m}\searrow\varepsilon}\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)^{\prime}_{b}=\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}

is a topological isomorphism, and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
is of compact type this way, i.e. it is the inductive limit of the
K
-Banach spaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m+1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ldots}

with compact, injective transition homomorphisms.

For the extreme case
\varepsilon=0
, the representation

P_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\times\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad(g,\ell)\longmapsto\ell(g^{-1}{\,\_\,}),

is locally analytic, and the underlying locally convex
K
-vector space
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
is of compact type analogously to (i).

Like in
Section

2.3
, we proceed step by step.

Lemma 2.6.2
.

Let
0<\varepsilon<1
with
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}\leq\varepsilon
(respectively,
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}<\varepsilon
), the group
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
acts on
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
(respectively, on
H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
) by continuous endomorphisms, and the topological isomorphism (
2.22
) is
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
-equivariant.
Moreover, the analogous assertions are true for
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
.

In the extreme case
\varepsilon=0
,
P_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
acts on
H^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
by continuous endomorphisms, and the topological isomorphism (
2.22
) is
P_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\},0}
-equivariant.

Proof.

For the local cohomology groups this follows from the discussion in
Section

2.2
.
The fact that the long exact sequence of local cohomology is equivariant for the respective group actions, implies the assertion for the kernels
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
,
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
, and
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{({\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
.
∎

Lemma 2.6.3
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\varepsilon\in{\lvert{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{K}}\rvert}
with
{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}<\varepsilon<1
, the representation

P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\times\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\,,\quad(g,v)\longmapsto g.v,

on the
K
-Banach space
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
is locally analytic.

Proof.

Like in
Lemma

2.3.4
the only assertion left to show is that the orbit maps

P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\,,\quad g\longmapsto g.v,

are locally analytic, for every
v\in\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
.
To do so, we first show that the orbit maps for the
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
-action on
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
are locally analytic.

Fix
g\in P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
and consider the affinoid subdomain
gD_{n}\subset{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(C)
, where we set
D_{n}\vcentcolon=1+{\pi}^{n}M_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{C})
, with the rigid analytic chart

\iota_{g}\colon D_{n}\longrightarrow gD_{n}\,,\quad h\longmapsto gh.

Moreover, we claim that, for every fixed
v\in\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
, the orbit map

P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}\longrightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\,,\quad h\longmapsto h.v,

restricted to
gD_{n}(K)
is given by a convergent power series.

To this end, we consider the admissible covering
{\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}
of
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}
whose Čech complex
C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})
computes
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
.
We fix a non-empty subset
I\subset\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}
and write
U\vcentcolon=U_{I,\varepsilon}
with the notation from (
2.15
).
We then have
h(U)=g(U)
, for all
h\in gD_{n}
.
Indeed, let
z=[z_{0}{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}z_{d}]\in U
and
(1+h^{\prime})\in D_{n}
so that

[z_{0}{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}z_{d}]\cdot(1+h^{\prime})=\vcentcolon[w_{0}{\,:\,}\ldots{\,:\,}w_{d}]\quad\text{, with $w_{i}=z_{i}+\sum_{j=0}^{d}z_{j}h^{\prime}_{ji}$.}

For
i\in I
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\lvert\sum_{j=0}^{d}z_{j}h^{\prime}_{ji}\Big\rvert\leq\max_{j=0}^{d}{\lvert{z_{j}h^{\prime}_{ji}}\rvert}\leq\max_{j=0}^{d}{\lvert{z_{j}}\rvert}\,{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}<\max_{j=0}^{d}\,\varepsilon\,{\lvert{z_{j}}\rvert}\leq{\lvert{z_{i}}\rvert}

where the last inequality holds because
z\in U\subset U_{i,\varepsilon}
.
This implies
{\lvert{w_{i}}\rvert}={\lvert{z_{i}}\rvert}
.
Now we compute, for
i\in I
,
j\in\{0,\ldots,d\}
:

\displaystyle\varepsilon\,{\lvert{w_{j}}\rvert}

\displaystyle\leq\max\Big(\varepsilon\,{\lvert{z_{j}}\rvert},\max_{k=0}^{d}\,\varepsilon\,{\lvert{z_{k}h^{\prime}_{kj}}\rvert}\Big)\leq\max\Big({\lvert{z_{i}}\rvert},\max_{k=0}^{d}\,\varepsilon\,{\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}\,{\lvert{z_{k}}\rvert}\Big)\leq{\lvert{z_{i}}\rvert}={\lvert{w_{i}}\rvert}

which implies that
(1+h^{\prime})^{-1}.z\in U
.

Since the above non-empty subset
I\subset\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}
was arbitrary, we obtain a map

gD_{n}(K)\times C^{i}(g({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon}),{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow C^{i}({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon},{\mathcal{E}})

that affords the
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{{r}}\}}
-action on
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
restricted to
gD_{n}(K)
, cf. (
2.5
).
Here
g({\mathcal{U}}_{\varepsilon})
denotes the translated covering
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-}=\bigcup_{i={r}+1}^{d}g(U_{i,\varepsilon})
.
Consequently it suffices to show that, for every
v\in{\mathcal{E}}(g(U))
with
U\vcentcolon=U_{I,\varepsilon}
, for non-empty
I\subset\{{r}+1,\ldots,d\}
, the map
gD_{n}(K)\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}(U)
,
h\mapsto h.v
, is given by a convergent power series.

For this we proceed similarly to the proof or
Lemma

2.3.4
.
Using that
h(U)=g(U)
, for all
h\in gD_{n}
, the group action
\sigma\colon{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,K}\times_{K}{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
induces the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{n}\times_{K}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces gD_{n}\times_{K}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{g}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{pr}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{pr}}_{2}}

Let
F_{v}\in{\mathcal{E}}(U)\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{(d+1)^{2}}\rangle
be the power series to which
v\in{\mathcal{E}}(g(U))
is mapped under

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(g(U))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{g}\times{\mathrm{id}})^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}(D_{n}\times_{K}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{g}\times{\mathrm{id}})^{\ast}{\mathrm{pr}}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}(D_{n}\times_{K}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong{\mathrm{pr}}_{2}^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}(D_{n}\times_{K}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong\big({\mathcal{O}}(D_{n}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathcal{O}}(U)\big)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}(U)}{\mathcal{E}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong{\mathcal{E}}(U)\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{(d+1)^{2}}\rangle.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{g}\times\mathrm{id})^{\ast}\Phi(D_{n}\times_{K}U)}

Now consider, for
h\in D_{n}(K)
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{n}\times_{K}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces gD_{n}\times_{K}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{g}\times{\mathrm{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h\times{\mathrm{id}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces gh}

In terms of
K
-affinoid algebras the morphism
h\times{\mathrm{id}}\colon U\rightarrow D_{n}\times_{K}U
is given by the evaluation homomorphism of power series

\displaystyle\mathrm{ev}_{h}\colon{\mathcal{O}}(D_{n}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}{\mathcal{O}}(U)\cong{\mathcal{O}}(U)\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{(d+1)^{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{O}}(U),

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\longmapsto F(h).

Hence we arrive at the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(g(U))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota_{g}\times{\mathrm{id}})^{\ast}\sigma^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}(D_{n}\times_{K}U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U)\langle T_{1},\ldots,T_{(d+1)^{2}}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(gh)^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}(U)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{E}}(U)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(h\times{\mathrm{id}})^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{h}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi_{gh}(U)}

which shows that
gh.v=F_{v}(h)
.

Having seen that the
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{r}\}}
-representation
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})
is locally analytic, we now consider the long exact sequence of local cohomology

\ldots\longrightarrow H^{i-1}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon)^{-},{\mathcal{E}})\xrightarrow{\partial^{i-1}_{\varepsilon}}H^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\ldots.

Since this sequence is
P^{n}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{r}\}}
-equivariant,
Proposition

1.3.8
(ii) implies that the kernel
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon)^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is a locally analytic representation, too.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

2.6.1
.

We argue similarly to the proof of
Proposition

2.3.1
.
We have seen in
Proposition

2.5.2
that the transition maps
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m+1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\rightarrow\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
are compact and have dense image.

Like in the proof of
Corollary

2.3.5
one deduces from
Lemma

2.6.3
that the contragredient representation on
\widetilde{H}^{i}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
is locally analytic, too.
The proposition then follows analogously.
∎

Remark 2.6.4
.

The cohomology groups
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
are finite-dimensional algebraic
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-representations.
It follows from
Corollary

1.7.7
that induced homomorphism
G\rightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}(H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}))
on
K
-valued points is a homomorphism of locally
K
-analytic Lie groups.
Therefore the
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
are locally analytic
G
-representations by
Proposition

1.3.10
.

3.
The
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(K)
-Representation
