H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})

Let
K
be a non-archimedean local field, and
{\mathcal{E}}
a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
.
Here we write
{\mathrm{\bf G}}={\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,K}
, and
G={\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(K)
for its associated locally
K
-analytic Lie group.

3.1.
Orlik’s Fundamental Complex and the Associated Spectral Sequence

In this section we want to recapitulate Orlik’s method
[
56
]
of using the geometric structure of the divisor at infinity
{\mathcal{Y}}\vcentcolon={\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K}\setminus{\mathcal{X}}
to obtain a filtration by locally analytic
G
-subrepresentations of
H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
, and to express the respective subquotients as extensions of certain locally analytic
G
-representations.
Since the reasoning introduced there for a
p
-adic field
K
carries over to the case of a general non-archimedean local field verbatim, we only present an overview.
At times we give some additional details but at others we refer to
[
56
]
for the full proofs.

The space of global sections
H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})
that we are interested in relates to the complement
{\mathcal{Y}}
via the long exact sequence of local cohomology

(3.1)

0\longrightarrow H^{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{1}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow 0.

Here the higher cohomology groups
H^{i}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})
, for
i>0
, vanish as
{\mathcal{X}}
is quasi-Stein.
Because the
H^{i}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
, for
i=0,1
, are finite-dimensional algebraic
G
-representations, the main difficulty lies in understanding
H^{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}})
.

In this regard the strategy of
[
56
]
unfolds as follows.
Let
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
denote the adic spaces attached to
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
and
{\mathcal{X}}
respectively.
Then one considers the complement

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}\vcentcolon=({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}\setminus{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ad}}

which is a closed pseudo-adic subspace of
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}
by
[
57
, Lemma 3.2]
, cf.
[
39
, Ch. 1.10]
.
Since the Zariski topoi of
{\mathcal{X}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
, and the ones of
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}=({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{rig}}
and
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}
are equivalent (see
[
38
, Prop. 4.5 (i)]
),
it follows that
H^{i}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}\big(({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}},{\mathcal{E}}\big)=H^{i}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
, for all
i\geq 0
.

Recall that, for a subset
I
of the set of simple roots
\Delta=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-1}\}
of
{\mathrm{\bf G}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,K}
, we denote the associated (lower) standard parabolic subgroup by
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I}
.
We write
P_{I}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I}(K)
,
P_{I,0}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
, and
P^{n}_{I}\vcentcolon=p^{-1}_{n}({\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}/{\pi}^{n}))
where

p_{n}\colon G_{0}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})\longrightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{K}/{\pi}^{n})

is the canonical reduction homomorphism.
Here
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I,{\mathbb{Z}}}
denotes the respective standard parabolic subgroup of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1,{\mathbb{Z}}}
.

For a subset
I\subsetneq\Delta
with
\Delta\setminus I=\{\alpha_{i_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{i_{s}}\}
,
i_{1}{\,<\,}\ldots{\,<\,}i_{s}
, we define the closed subvariety

Y_{I}\vcentcolon={\mathbb{P}}_{K}\bigg(\bigoplus_{j=0}^{i_{1}}K\cdot e_{j}\bigg)={\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{i_{1}}=V_{+}(X_{i_{1}+1},\ldots,X_{d})\subset{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}

so that

{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}=\bigcup_{I\subsetneq\Delta}\bigcup_{g\in G/P_{I}}gY_{I}^{\mathrm{ad}}.

Moreover, for a compact open subset
W\subset G/P_{I}
, we consider

Z_{I}^{W}\vcentcolon=\bigcup_{g\in W}gY_{I}^{\mathrm{ad}}

which is a closed pseudo-adic subspace of
({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}
, see
[
57
, Lemma 3.2]
.
In particular, we have
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}=Z^{G/P_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{d-1}\}}}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{d-1}\}}
.

Next one defines on
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
certain étale sheaves of locally constant sections supported on
Z_{I}^{G/P_{I}}
.
To this end, let

\displaystyle\Phi_{I,g}

\displaystyle\colon gY_{I}^{\mathrm{ad}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}

\displaystyle\Psi_{I,W}

\displaystyle\colon Z_{I}^{W}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}

be the embeddings of closed pseudo-adic spaces and consider the étale sheaves

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}_{g,I}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(\Phi_{I,g})_{\ast}(\Phi_{I,g})^{\ast}{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}_{Z^{W}_{I}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=(\Psi_{I,W})_{\ast}(\Psi_{I,W})^{\ast}{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}

where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
denotes the constant étale sheaf on
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
with stalks equal to
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Furthermore, we let
{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}
denote the category of disjoint coverings of
G/P_{I}
by compact open subsets with morphisms given by refinement.
For a covering
c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}
of the form
G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{j\in A}W_{j}
, let
{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}
denote the image of the sheaf homomorphism

\bigoplus\limits_{j\in A}{\mathbb{Z}}_{Z^{W_{j}}_{I}}\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow\prod\limits_{g\in G/P_{I}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{g,I}

which is induced by the homomorphisms
{\mathbb{Z}}_{Z_{I}^{W_{j}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{g,I}
, for
g\in W_{j}
, cf.
[
56
, p. 621]
.
Taking the inductive limit over all coverings of
{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}
one arrives at the aforementioned sheaf

\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}

of locally constant sections supported on
Z_{I}^{G/P_{I}}
with values in
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

With the appropriate sheaf homomorphisms given by restriction, these sheaves fit together to yield a complex of sheaves on
{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}

(3.2)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=1\end{subarray}}\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\ldots\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=i\end{subarray}}\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\longrightarrow\ldots

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=d-1\end{subarray}}\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\longrightarrow\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{\emptyset}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\longrightarrow 0.

Theorem 3.1.1

(
[
56
, Thm. 2.1.1]
)
.

The complex (
3.2
) is acyclic.

Now, let
0\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I}}^{0}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I}}^{1}\rightarrow\ldots
be an injective resolution of the
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}}
-module
{\mathcal{E}}
.
Let
\iota\colon{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d})^{\mathrm{ad}}
denote the closed embedding.
We want to consider the double complex obtained by applying
{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{\ast}({\,\_\,}),{\mathcal{I}}^{q})
to the acyclic resolution (
3.2
) of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}
, i.e.

(3.3)

E_{0}^{p,q}\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\mathrm{Hom}}\Bigg(\iota_{\ast}\bigg(\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\end{subarray}}\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\bigg),{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\Bigg)&\text{, if $-(d-1)\leq p\leq 0$, $q\geq 0$,}\\
0&\text{, else.}\end{cases}

This double complex is concentrated in the upper left quadrant.

There is a natural action of
G
on
E_{0}^{\bullet,\bullet}
as follows:
For fixed
g\in G
and
I\subsetneq\Delta
, we have a homomorphism by functoriality of taking the inverse image under the automorphism
g

(3.4)

{\mathrm{Hom}}\bigg(\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big),{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\bigg)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Hom}}\bigg(g^{-1}\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big),g^{-1}{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\bigg).

Moreover, for the restriction
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{g}\colon{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}
of
g
we have
{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{g}}^{-1}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}={\mathbb{Z}}_{g^{-1}c}
where
g^{-1}c
denotes the translated covering
G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{j\in A}g^{-1}W_{j}
, for
c=\{W_{j}\mid j\in A\}
.
This yields

g^{-1}\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big)\cong\iota_{\ast}{\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{g}}^{-1}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big)\cong\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{g^{-1}c}\Big).

Together with the homomorphism
g^{-1}{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\rightarrow g^{\ast}{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\rightarrow{\mathcal{I}}^{q}
induced from
\Phi_{g}\colon g^{\ast}{\mathcal{E}}\rightarrow{\mathcal{E}}
in the second component we obtain a homomorphism

(3.5)

{\mathrm{Hom}}\bigg(g^{-1}\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big),g^{-1}{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\bigg)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Hom}}\bigg(\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{g^{-1}c}\Big),{\mathcal{I}}^{q}\bigg).

Then
g
acts via the endomorphism that arises as the composition of (
3.4
) and (
3.5
).

Associated with the double complex (
3.3
) we have two spectral sequences
{}^{\rm h}\!E^{p,q}_{r}
and
{}^{\rm v}\!E^{p,q}_{r}
.
Since, for every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, there are only finitely many pairs
(p,q)\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{2}
with
p+q=n
and
E^{p,q}_{0}\neq 0
, both spectral sequences converge to the total cohomology of the double complex
[
77
,
Tag 0132
]
.
As all rows of
{}^{\rm h}\!E^{p,q}_{0}
are exact apart from the entry at
p=0
, we compute

{}^{\rm h}\!E_{1}^{p,q}=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{\ast}{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}},{\mathcal{I}}^{q})&\text{, if $p=0$, $q\geq 0$,}\\
0&\text{, else.}\end{cases}

Therefore
{}^{\rm h}\!E_{2}^{p,q}
reads as follows

{}^{\rm h}\!E_{2}^{p,q}=\begin{cases}{\mathrm{Ext}}^{q}(\iota_{\ast}{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{, if $p=0$, $q\geq 0$,}\\
0&\text{, else,}\end{cases}

and the spectral sequence
{}^{\rm h}\!E_{r}^{p,q}
collapses at the second page.
Moreover, we have the
G
-equivariant isomorphism

{}^{\rm h}\!E_{2}^{0,q}={\mathrm{Ext}}^{q}(\iota_{\ast}{\mathbb{Z}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}},{\mathcal{E}})=H^{q}_{{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{ad}}}\big(({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K})^{\mathrm{ad}},{\mathcal{E}}\big)=H^{q}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{P}}^{d}_{K},{\mathcal{E}}),

for all
q\geq 0
, by
[
33
, Prop. 2.3 bis.]
.

We now turn to the spectral sequence
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{r}^{p,q}
and compute that

{}^{\rm v}\!E^{p,q}_{1}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ext}}^{q}\Bigg(\iota_{\ast}\bigg(\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\end{subarray}}\varinjlim\limits_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\bigg),{\mathcal{E}}\Bigg)&\text{, if $-(d-1)\leq p\leq 0$, $q\geq 0$,}\\
0&\text{, else.}\end{cases}

Furthermore, for all
I\subsetneq\Delta
, it is shown in
[
56
, Prop. 2.2.1]
that there is an isomorphism

(3.6)

{\mathrm{Ext}}^{q}\bigg(\iota_{\ast}\Big(\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}\Big),{\mathcal{E}}\bigg)=\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0}/P_{I}^{n}}H^{q}_{gY_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

for all
q\geq 0
, with the definition of the “open”
\varepsilon_{n}
-neighbourhood of
Y_{I}
from (
2.11
).
Here and in the following, we abbreviate
\varepsilon_{n}\vcentcolon={\lvert{{\pi}}\rvert}^{n}
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Remark 3.1.2
.

We want to explain how the
G
-action on the
{\mathrm{Ext}}
-groups on the left hand side of (
3.6
) transfers to the right hand side.
While doing so, we will introduce some useful notation.

First note that
G/P_{I}\cong G_{0}/P_{I,0}
by the Iwasawa decomposition (see
[
16
, §3.5]
).
The proof of the isomorphism (
3.6
) uses that the family of coverings

G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{g\in G_{0}/P^{n}_{I}}gP_{I}^{n}/P_{I}\quad\text{, with $gP_{I}^{n}/P_{I}\vcentcolon=\big\{gpP_{I}\in G/P_{I}\mid p\in P^{n}_{I}\big\}$, }

for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, is cofinal in
{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}
.
This shows that

\varinjlim_{c\in{\mathcal{C}}_{G/P_{I}}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{c}=\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0}/P_{I}^{n}}{\mathbb{Z}}_{Z_{I}^{gP^{n}_{I}}}.

One proceeds by applying
[
33
, Prop. 2.3 bis.]
and arguing that certain higher derived inverse limits vanish.

Then the isomorphism (
3.6
) is
G
-equivariant when the right hand side is equipped with the following
G
-action by continuous endomorphisms:
For fixed
g\in G
, to give an endomorphism by which
g
acts it suffices to define compatible homomorphisms

(3.7)

\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0}/P_{I}^{n}}H^{q}_{gY_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s_{m}}H^{q}_{g_{i}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

for all
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Here the
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{m}}
are some coset representatives of
G_{0}/P_{I}^{m}
so that we have
G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}P_{I}^{m}/P_{I}
.

We choose
n=n(g,m)\geq m
to be large enough such that the covering
G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{s_{n}}h_{j}P^{n}_{I}/P_{I}
, for coset representatives
h_{1},\ldots,h_{s_{n}}
of
G_{0}/P^{n}_{I}
, is a refinement of the translated covering
G/P_{I}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{m}}gg_{i}P^{m}_{I}/P_{I}
.
In this way we obtain a surjection

\sigma_{g}\colon\{1,\ldots,s_{n}\}\longrightarrow\{1,\ldots,s_{m}\}

where
\sigma_{g}(j)
is defined via
h_{j}P^{n}_{I}/P_{I}\subset gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}_{I}/P_{I}
.

One computes that

h_{j}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})=h_{j}P^{n}_{I}.Y_{I}\subset gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}_{I}.(P_{I}.Y_{I})=gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})

Then the homomorphisms

\varphi_{g}\colon H^{q}_{h_{j}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})

from (
2.6
) give

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{j=1}^{s_{n}}H^{q}_{h_{j}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s_{m}}H^{q}_{g_{i}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

\displaystyle(v_{1},\ldots,v_{s_{n}})

\displaystyle\longmapsto\Big(\sum_{j\in\sigma_{g}^{-1}(\{1\})}\varphi_{g}(v_{j}),\ldots,\sum_{j\in\sigma_{g}^{-1}(\{s_{m}\})}\varphi_{g}(v_{j})\Big).

Combining this with the projection

\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0}/P_{I}^{n}}H^{q}_{gY_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{j=1}^{s_{n}}H^{q}_{h_{j}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n(g,m)})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})

yields the sought homomorphism (
3.7
).
One checks that these homomorphisms are compatible and do not depend on the choice of
n
.
∎

Note that when
g\in G_{0}
, the
n
for the action of
g
can always be chosen to be
n=m
.
In this case,
g
even acts on each constituent of projective limit in (
3.6
) individually.

Using the isomorphisms

\varphi_{g_{i}}\colon H^{q}_{g_{i}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

for coset representatives
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{m}}
of
G_{0}/P_{I}^{m}
, we obtain a
G_{0}
-equivariant isomorphism

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s_{m}}H^{q}_{g_{i}Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P_{I}^{m}}^{G_{0}}\Big(H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)\,,\quad(v_{1},\ldots,v_{s_{m}})\longmapsto\sum_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}\bullet\varphi_{g_{i}}(v_{i}).

Consequently the spectral sequence reads as follows

{}^{\rm v}\!E_{1}^{p,q}=\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\end{subarray}}\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{I}^{n}}\Big(H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)\Rightarrow{}^{\rm v}\!E_{\infty}^{p+q}={}^{\rm h}\!E_{\infty}^{p+q}=H^{p+q}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}).

One continues analysing this spectral sequence by considering complexes
K^{\bullet}_{q,n}
defined as

K^{p}_{q,n}\vcentcolon=\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\end{subarray}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{I}^{n}}\Big(H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big),

for
-(d-1)\leq p\leq 0
,
q\geq 0
, so that
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{1}^{\bullet,q}=\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\bullet}_{q,n}
.
Applying
Proposition

2.5.7
to the local cohomology groups with respect to
Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})={\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{i_{1}}
, for
I\subsetneq\Delta
with
\Delta\setminus I=\{\alpha_{i_{1}},\ldots,\alpha_{i_{s}}\}
,
i_{1}{\,<\,}\ldots{\,<\,}i_{s}
, and
q\geq 0
, we find that

H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})=\begin{cases}0&\text{, if $\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\}{\not\subset}I$,}\\
H^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{, if $\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\}\subset I$ and $\alpha_{d-q}\notin I$,}\\
H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})&\text{, if $\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q}\}\subset I$.}\end{cases}

In particular the complex
K^{\bullet}_{q,n}
is concentrated in the degrees
p=-q+1,\ldots,0
.

The next step is to write
K^{\bullet}_{q,n}
as an extension of complexes
K^{\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
and
K^{\prime\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
via the
G
-equivariant, short strictly exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\prime p}_{q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vcentcolon=\joinrel=\joinrel=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip-17.07182pt\displaystyle\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\\
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\in I\\
\alpha_{d-q}\notin I\end{subarray}}\hskip-17.07182pt{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{I}^{n}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{q,n}^{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=\joinrel=\joinrel=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip-17.07182pt\displaystyle\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\\
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\in I\end{subarray}}\hskip-17.07182pt{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{I}^{n}}\Big(H^{q}_{Y_{I}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K^{\prime\prime p}_{q,n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vcentcolon=\joinrel=\joinrel=}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hskip-17.07182pt\displaystyle\bigoplus\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\subset\Delta\\
{\lvert{\Delta\setminus I}\rvert}=-p+1\\
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\in I\end{subarray}}\hskip-17.07182pt{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{I}^{n}}\big(H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

of
K
-Fréchet spaces.
This sequence is induced by the short strictly exact sequences

0\longrightarrow 0\longrightarrow H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow 0,

for
I\subsetneq\Delta
with
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q}\in I
, and by

0\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow 0,

for
I\subsetneq\Delta
with
\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\in I
,
\alpha_{d-q}\notin I
.
Here the second homomorphism in the latter sequence is surjective as
H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}\setminus{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n}),{\mathcal{E}})=0
.

By
Proposition

2.5.2
the projective systems
(K^{\prime p}_{q,n})_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
satisfy the topological Mittag-Leffler condition of
Lemma

2.5.4
.
Therefore we obtain the
G_{0}
-equivariant, short strictly exact sequence

(3.8)

0\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\bullet}_{q,n}\longrightarrow{}^{\rm v}\!E_{1}^{\bullet,q}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime\bullet}_{q,n}\longrightarrow 0

of complexes of
K
-Fréchet spaces.

One finds that the complexes
K^{\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
and
K^{\prime\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
, for all
q\geq 0
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, are acyclic aside from the very left and right position
[
56
, Lemma 2.2.5]
.
After checking the (topological) Mittag-Leffler conditions one then concludes that the complexes
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
and
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
are acyclic apart from the very left and right position as well
[
56
, Rmk. 2.2.6]
.
To compute
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{p,q}
we can consider the long exact sequence of the cohomology of the complexes (
3.8
).
It follows that the only non-vanishing terms of
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{p,q}=H^{p}\big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\bullet}_{p,n}\big)
are the ones for
p=-q+1
,
q=1,\ldots,d
, and the ones for
p=0
,
q\geq 2
.
For these terms we obtain a short strictly exact sequence

(3.9)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle K^{\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\Big)\longrightarrow{}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{-q+1,q}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\Big)\longrightarrow 0,

for
q=1,\ldots,d
, and one shows that

{}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{0,q}=H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

for
q\geq 2
.
Moreover, from this one concludes that the spectral sequence
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{r}^{p,q}
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page
[
56
, p. 633]
.

We want to investigate the strong dual of (
3.9
).
We first look at
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime p}_{q,n}
.
Since each
K^{\prime\prime p}_{q,n}
is finite-dimensional, the transition homomorphisms of
\big(K^{\prime\prime p}_{q,n}\big)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
are compact
[
67
, Lemma 16.4]
so that
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime p}_{q,n}
is a nuclear
K
-Fréchet space
[
67
, Prop. 19.9]
.
Then
{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)
is a nuclear
K
-Fréchet space, too
[
67
, Prop. 19.4 (i)]
.
It follows that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\Big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\cong\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)\Big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}

by
[
67
, Prop. 16.5]
.
As
K^{\prime\prime\bullet}_{q,n}
is exact at
K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}
, the image of
K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}
is closed and this homomorphism is strict
[
11
, IV. §4.2 Thm. 1]
.
Hence
[
11
, IV. §4.1 Prop. 2]
implies that there is topological isomorphism

{\mathrm{Ker}}\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong{\mathrm{Coker}}\Big(\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\Big).

To simplify the notation we write
{\mathrm{\bf Q}}_{d-q}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{I_{d-q}}
for the standard parabolic subgroup corresponding to the subset
I_{d-q}=\{\alpha_{0},\ldots,\alpha_{d-q-1}\}\subset\Delta
.
In Remark
1.5.5
(i) we have seen that taking the strong dual and finite induction commute with each other.
Therefore, we obtain a
G
-equivariant, topological isomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Coker}}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\big(K^{\prime\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\Big)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\big(H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\big)\bigg/\sum_{i=d-q}^{d-1}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}\big(H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\cong\Big(H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(K)\Big)\bigg/\sum_{i=d-q}^{d-1}H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(K)

\displaystyle\cong H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}v^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}

where

(3.10)

v^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(K)\bigg/\sum_{i=d-q}^{d-1}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(K).

We have used the push-pull formula from Remark
1.5.5
(ii), since
H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
already is a
G
-representation, and that taking the projective tensor product with
H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
is exact
[
13
, Lemma 2.1 (ii)]
.

Furthermore,
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.32 (i)]
yields

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Big(H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}v^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big)\cong H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Big(v^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big).

The second factor of this tensor product can be expressed as a
smooth generalized Steinberg representation
(cf.
[
17
]
)

v^{G}_{P_{I}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{I}}(K)\bigg/\sum_{I\subsetneq J\subset\Delta}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{J}}(K),

for
I\subset\Delta
.
Here
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{I}}(K)
denotes the smooth induction of the trivial
P_{I}
-representation, i.e. the space locally constant functions invariant under
P_{I}
endowed with left regular
G
-action:

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{I}}(K)\vcentcolon=\big\{f\in C^{\mathrm{sm}}(G,K)\,\big|\,\forall g\in G,p\in P_{I}:f(gp)=f(g)\big\}.

Lemma 3.1.3
.

For
I\subset\Delta
, there is a
G
-equivariant, topological isomorphism

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\bigg({\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}(K)\bigg/\sum_{I\subsetneq J\subset\Delta}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{J}^{n}}(K)\bigg)\cong v^{G}_{P_{I}}

where
v^{G}_{P_{I}}
denotes the generalized smooth Steinberg representation endowed with the finest locally convex topology.

Proof.

For all
J\subset\Delta
and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have a well-defined,
G_{0}
-equivariant inclusion

(3.11)

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{J}^{n}}(K)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P_{J}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}(K)\,,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{s_{n}}g_{i}\bullet\lambda_{i}\longmapsto\big[g\mapsto\lambda_{i}\text{ , if $gP_{J}\in g_{i}P_{J}^{n}/P_{J}$}\big].

Here
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{n}}
are coset representatives of
G_{0}/P^{n}_{J}
.
Since
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{J}^{n}}(K)
is finite dimensional, this is a continuous homomorphism when the right hand side carries the finest locally convex topology.

Moreover, given a locally constant function
f\colon G\rightarrow K
in
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{J}}(K)
, there exists
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for the covering
G/P_{J}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{n}}g_{i}P^{n}_{J}/P_{J}
the function
f
is constant on each open subset
g_{i}P^{n}_{J}\cdot P_{J}
.
Thus
f
is contained in the image of (
3.11
).
With the choice of the finest locally convex topology on
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{J}}(K)
it follows that
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{J}}(K)\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{J}}(K)
is a topological isomorphism.

With
v^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}
defined in the obvious way, we take the inductive limit over the diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\bigoplus_{I\subsetneq J\subset\Delta}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P_{J}^{n}}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\displaystyle\bigoplus_{I\subsetneq J\subset\Delta}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{J}}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{sm}},G}_{P_{I}}(K)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v^{G}_{P_{I}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

which have strictly exact rows.
The snake lemma
A.13
then shows that the induced
G_{0}
-equivariant homomorphism
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}v^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}\rightarrow v^{G}_{P_{I}}
is a topological isomorphism.
Moreover, this isomorphism is
G
-equivariant when
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}v^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I}}
carries the
G
-action induced from
Remark

3.1.2
.
∎

We now turn towards
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime p}_{q,n}
.
Here the transition homomorphisms of
\big(K^{\prime p}_{q,n}\big)_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}
are compact by
Corollary

2.5.6
so that
\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime p}_{q,n}
is a nuclear
K
-Fréchet space, too.
Similarly to before one finds that

{\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Coker}}\Big(\big(K^{\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\big(K^{\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\Big)

with injective, compact transition homomorphisms in the inductive limit of the right hand side.

We write
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{d-q}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{J_{d-q}}
for the standard parabolic subgroup corresponding to the subset
J_{d-q}\vcentcolon=\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{d-q}\}\subset\Delta
.
We recall from
Proposition

2.6.1
(ii) that
W_{n}\vcentcolon=\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
is a locally analytic
P^{n}_{d-q}
-representation.
Using the exactness of the “finite” induction
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}({\,\_\,})
, the push-pull formula from Remark
1.5.5
(ii), and exactness of tensoring with
W_{n}

[
13
, Lemma 2.1 (ii)]
, we compute that

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Coker}}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(K^{\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\rightarrow\big(K^{\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\big)^{\prime}_{b}\Big)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(W_{n})\Bigg/\sum_{i=d-q+1}^{d-1}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(W_{n})

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Bigg({\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(W_{n})\Bigg/\sum_{i=d-q+1}^{d-1}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(W_{n})\Bigg)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Bigg(\Big(W_{n}\otimes_{K}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(K)\Big)\Bigg/\sum_{i=d-q+1}^{d-1}W_{n}\otimes_{K}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(K)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Big(W_{n}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big)

with the finite-dimensional representations

v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}(K)\Bigg/\sum_{i=d-q+1}^{d-1}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{P^{n}_{I_{d-q}\cup\{\alpha_{i}\}}}(K).

Remark 3.1.4
.

We want to describe the induced
G
-action on

{\mathrm{Ker}}\Big(\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime-q+1}_{q,n}\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}K^{\prime-q+2}_{q,n}\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big).

To ease the notation, we write
{\mathrm{\bf P}}={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{d-q}
, and
{\mathrm{\bf Q}}={\mathrm{\bf Q}}_{d-q}
here.
Fix
g\in G
and consider an element
v
of the right hand side term.
Let
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that

v=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\in{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{m}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}\Big),

where
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{m}}
are coset representatives of
G_{0}/P^{m}
.

Similarly to
Remark

3.1.2
, let
n\geq m
such that
G/Q=\bigcup_{j=1}^{s_{n}}h_{j}Q^{n}/Q
is a refinement of the translated covering
G/Q=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{m}}gg_{i}Q^{m}/Q
, for coset representatives
h_{1},\ldots,h_{s_{n}}
of
G_{0}/Q^{n}
.
We can consider the induced coverings of
G/P
under the surjection
G/Q\rightarrow G/P
.
After enlarging
n
we may assume that the induced covering
G/P=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{n}}h_{j}P^{n}/P
is a refinement of the induced covering
G/P=\bigcup_{i=1}^{s_{m}}gg_{i}P^{m}/P
.

For
j=1,\ldots,s_{n}
, we have
h_{j}Q^{n}/Q\subset gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}Q^{m}/Q
with the notation of
Remark

3.1.2
.
We claim that this implies
h_{j}P^{n}/Q\subset gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}/Q
.
Indeed, using

\displaystyle h_{j}P^{n}/Q=\bigcup_{h_{j^{\prime}}P^{n}=h_{j}P^{n}}h_{j^{\prime}}Q^{n}/Q\subset\bigcup_{h_{j^{\prime}}P^{n}=h_{j}P^{n}}gg_{\sigma_{g}(j^{\prime})}Q^{m}/Q\subset\bigcup_{h_{j^{\prime}}P^{n}=h_{j}P^{n}}gg_{\sigma_{g}(j^{\prime})}P^{m}/Q

it suffices to show that
g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}=g_{\sigma_{g}(j^{\prime})}P^{m}
if
h_{j}P^{n}=h_{j^{\prime}}P^{n}
.
For this, we compute that
h_{j}Q^{n}/P\subset h_{j}P^{n}/P\cap gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}/P
.
By the assumption on
n
with respect to the covering of
G/P
this shows that
h_{j}P^{n}/P\subset gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}/P

9
9

9

Recall that the function
\sigma_{g}
is defined with regard to the coverings of
G/Q
.
.
The, if
h_{j}P^{n}=h_{j^{\prime}}P^{n}
, the sets
gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}/P
and
gg_{\sigma_{g}(j^{\prime})}P^{m}/P
have non-empty intersection which implies
g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}=g_{\sigma_{g}(j^{\prime})}P^{m}
.

We now set
p_{g,j}\vcentcolon=h^{-1}_{j}gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}
so that we have
P^{n}/Q\subset p_{g,j}P^{m}/Q
.
Then
g
induces continuous homomorphisms

\varphi_{p_{g,j}}^{t}\colon\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}.

Furthermore, we have
p_{g,j}^{-1}P^{n}\subset P^{m}\cdot Q
.
Written in terms of locally constant functions
p_{g,j}
gives a continuous homomorphism

\displaystyle{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}(K)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K),

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\longmapsto f(p_{g,j}^{-1}{\,\_\,})=\big[p\mapsto f(p^{\prime})\text{ , if $p_{g,j}^{-1}p=p^{\prime}q$, for $p^{\prime}\in P^{m}$, $q\in Q$}\big].

This in turn yields a continuous homomorphism
\psi_{p_{g,j}}\colon v^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}\rightarrow v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}
.
We let
\tau_{p_{g,j}}
denote the tensor product

\tau_{p_{g,j}}\vcentcolon=\varphi^{t}_{p_{g,j}}\otimes\psi_{p_{g,j}}\colon\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{m})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}.

In total the homomorphism by which
g
acts is given by

g.\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\bigg)=\sum_{j=1}^{s_{n}}h_{j}\bullet\tau_{p_{g,j}}(v_{\sigma_{g}(j)})\in{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\Big).

∎

Since the spectral sequence
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{r}^{p,q}
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page, we obtain a filtration of
H^{1}_{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
by
G
-invariant subspaces whose successive subquotients are isomorphic to
{}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{-q+1,q}
.
More precisely and taking into account the long exact sequence (
3.1
) of local cohomology with respect to
{\mathcal{Y}}\subset{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
, we arrive at the following theorem.

Theorem 3.1.5

(cf.
[
56
, Thm. 2.2.8]
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
.
Then there exist a filtration by closed
D(G)
-submodules

H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})=V^{d}\supset V^{d-1}\supset\ldots\supset V^{1}\supset V^{0}=H^{0}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}),

and, for
q=1,\ldots,d
, short strictly exact sequences of locally analytic
G
-representations

(3.12)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle H^{q}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{G}_{Q_{d-q}}\longrightarrow\big(V^{q}/V^{q-1}\big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big)\longrightarrow 0.

When
K
is a
p
-adic field, Orlik analyses the right hand side term of (
3.12
) further.
In
[
56
, p. 634]
he shows that there is an isomorphism of locally analytic
G
-representations

(3.13)

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big)\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{P_{d-q}}\Big(N^{\prime}_{d-q}\otimes v^{P_{d-q}}_{Q_{d-q}}\Big)^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{d-q}}.

We explain the notation used here.
Let
U({\mathfrak{g}})
and
U({\mathfrak{p}}_{d-q})
denote the universal enveloping algebras of the Lie algebras of
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
and
{\mathrm{\bf P}}_{d-q}
respectively.
One shows that
\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
is a quotient of a generalized Verma module for
U({\mathfrak{g}})
.
More precisely, there exists a finite-dimensional
P_{d-q}
-subrepresentation
N_{d-q}\subset\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
which generates it as a
U({\mathfrak{g}})
-module
[
56
, Lemma 1.2.1]
, i.e. there exists an epimorphism of
U({\mathfrak{g}})
-modules

U({\mathfrak{g}})\otimes_{U({\mathfrak{p}}_{d-q})}N_{d-q}\relbar\joinrel\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}).

Let
{\mathfrak{d}}_{d-q}
denote the kernel of this epimorphism.
Then
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{P_{d-q}}\big(N^{\prime}_{d-q}\otimes v^{P_{d-q}}_{Q_{d-q}}\big)^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{d-q}}
indicates the subspace of those functions in the locally analytic induction that are annihilated by
{\mathfrak{d}}_{d-q}
, cf.
[
56
, p. 607]
.
In particular, the right hand side of (
3.13
) does not depend on the choice of
N_{d-q}
.

However, when
K
is of positive characteristic
\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})
in general is no longer finitely generated, even if we replace
U({\mathfrak{g}})
by the algebraic distribution algebra
{\rm Dist}({\mathrm{\bf G}})\cong{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
of
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
, see
[
50
, Ch. 2.3]
.
We tackle this problem by adapting a different description of
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G}_{P_{d-q}}\big(N^{\prime}_{d-q}\otimes v^{P_{d-q}}_{Q_{d-q}}\big)^{{\mathfrak{d}}_{d-q}}
via the functors
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}^{G}
defined by Orlik and Strauch in
[
58
]
.
In
Section

3.4
we then compare Orlik’s and our description for the case of a
p
-adic field.

3.2.
The Subquotients of
H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
as Locally Analytic
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
-Representations

We now want to analyse the
G
-representations that occur as a quotient of
\big({}^{\rm v}\!E_{2}^{-q+1,q}\big)^{\prime}_{b}
in (
3.12
).
We change the notation in as much as we fix
{r}\vcentcolon=d-q\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}
, and write
{\mathrm{\bf P}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{d-q}
as well as
{\mathrm{\bf Q}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf Q}}_{d-q}
.
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we have seen in the proof of
Proposition

2.6.1
that

(3.14)

V_{n}\vcentcolon=\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K)

is a locally analytic
P^{n}
-representation whose underlying locally convex vector space is a
K
-Banach space.

Lemma 3.2.1
.

The transition homomorphisms
V_{n}\rightarrow V_{n+1}
are injective and compact, i.e.

V\vcentcolon=\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}V_{n}

is of compact type this way.
In particular via the
P^{n}
-actions on the
V_{n}
,
V
becomes a locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P_{0})
-module in the sense of
Definition

1.6.18
.

Proof.

We have seen in
Proposition

2.5.2
that the transition maps

\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n+1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})

are compact and have dense image.
Therefore their transposes are injective and compact by
[
67
, Lemma 16.4]
.
Moreover the homomorphisms
v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\rightarrow v^{P^{n+1}}_{Q^{n+1}}
which are induced by the restriction map
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K)\rightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n+1}}_{Q^{n+1}}(K),\,f\mapsto f{\!\,\mid_{P^{n+1}}}
, are injective as well.
Hence it follows from
[
27
, Cor. 1.1.27]
that the tensor product
V_{n}\rightarrow V_{n+1}
of these maps is injective.
The homomorphism
v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\rightarrow v^{P^{n+1}}_{Q^{n+1}}
is compact as a homomorphism between finite-dimensional
K
-vector spaces by
[
67
, Lemma 16.4]
.
Therefore
[
67
, Lemma 18.12]
implies that
V_{n}\rightarrow V_{n+1}
is compact as well.

Finally, we have
{\mathrm{hy}}(P^{n})={\mathrm{hy}}(G)
because
P^{n}\subset G
is an open subgroup. Hence each
V_{n}
is a locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P^{n})
-module via
Remark

1.6.19
, and therefore a locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P_{0})
-module in particular.
It follows that
V
is a locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P_{0})
-module as well. ∎

Remark 3.2.2
.

In fact,
V
is even a locally analytic
P
-representation.
For fixed
p\in P
, we find
n\geq m
large enough such that
h_{j}P^{n}/Q\subset pg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}P^{m}/Q
, for all
j=1,\ldots,s_{n}
, like in
Remark

3.1.4
.
Considering the images under
G/Q\rightarrow G/P
, it follows from
1\in p^{-1}P^{n}/P
that
P^{n}/P\subset pP^{m}/P
.
This shows that
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}\subset p{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}
and we obtain a homomorphism

\varphi_{p}^{t}\colon\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{m})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}.

Moreover, we have the continuous homomorphism
\psi_{p}\colon v^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}\rightarrow v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}
induced by

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{m}}_{Q^{m}}(K)\longrightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f(p^{-1}{\,\_\,}).

The tensor product of these yields the continuous homomorphism
\tau_{p}=(\varphi_{p}^{t}\otimes\psi_{p})\colon V_{m}\rightarrow V_{n}
.
Like before the collection of these
\tau_{p}
, for all
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
, gives the action of
p
on
V
.
This
P
-action extends the one of
P_{0}
and
V
is a locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P)
-module this way.
∎

Lemma 3.2.3
.

There is a canonical topological isomorphism of locally analytic
({\mathrm{hy}}(G),P)
-modules

V\cong\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{{r}})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)}_{B_{d-{r}}}.

Here
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r},K}
is viewed as a subgroup of the standard Levi factor
{\mathrm{\bf L}}\cong{\mathrm{GL}}_{{r}+1,K}\times_{K}{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r},K}
of
{\mathrm{\bf P}}={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{\Delta\setminus\{\alpha_{r}\}}
, and
{\mathrm{\bf B}}_{d-{r}}
denotes the standard (lower) Borel subgroup of
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r},K}
.
On
v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)}_{B_{d-{r}}}
the group
P
acts via inflation,
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
acts trivially, and it carries the finest locally convex topology.

Proof.

First note that we have an isomorphism

{\mathrm{Ind}}^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}_{B_{d-{r}}^{n}}(K)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f{\!\,\mid_{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}},

since
P^{n}/Q^{n}\cong{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})/B_{d-{r}}^{n}
.
Here we view
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})\subset P_{0}
as a subgroup.
This yields isomorphisms
v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\cong v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})}_{B_{d-{r}}^{n}}
, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Taking the inductive limit over these isomorphisms and applying
Lemma

3.1.3
to the case of
B_{d-{r}}\subset{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)
, we obtain an isomorphism
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\cong v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)}_{B_{d-{r}}}
of locally convex
K
-vector spaces.
Moreover, one computes that this isomorphism is
P
-equivariant when
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}
carries the
P
-action from
Remark

3.2.2
, and
v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)}_{B_{d-{r}}}
the one by inflation.

We recall from
Proposition

2.6.1
(ii) that
\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
.
By
[
27
, Prop. 1.1.32]
we then obtain a
P
-equivariant topological isomorphism

(3.15)

V=\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}{\,\otimes_{K}\,}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\Big)\cong\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{d-{r}}(K)}_{B_{d-{r}}}.

Finally note that
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
acts trivially on
v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}
, for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, as
Q^{n}\subset P^{n}
is an open subgroup.
On
\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
the
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
-action is induced by the actions on the
\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
.
Therefore (
3.15
) is
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
-equivariant.
∎

We come back to the locally analytic
G
-representation
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(W_{n})
from (
3.12
), for
W_{n}\vcentcolon=\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{r}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}(K)
.
The
K
-Banach spaces
V_{n}
allow us to exhibit this as a locally convex
K
-vector space of compact type.

Lemma 3.2.4
.

There is a canonical topological isomorphism of locally analytic
G
-representations

(3.16)

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(W_{n})\cong\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n}).

Here the
G
-action on
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})
is given in the way analogous to
Remark

3.1.4
.
Moreover, the transition maps
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\rightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n+1}}(V_{n+1})
of the right hand side are compact and injective so that the underlying locally convex
K
-vector space of the above
G
-representation is of compact type.

Proof.

In view of the inductive limit description of
Proposition

2.6.1
, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, the homomorphism

\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n+1})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}

factors over
\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}
.
We therefore obtain a commutative diagram of locally analytic
P^{n+1}
-representations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n-1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n+1})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}.}

Combined with the canonical homomorphisms
v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\rightarrow v^{P^{n+1}}_{Q^{n+1}}
, this gives factorizations of the transition maps of both inductive limits in (
3.16
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n-1}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n-1})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n-1}}_{Q^{n-1}}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})^{-}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\Big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n+1}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{d-{r}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{r}_{K}(\varepsilon_{n+1})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n+1}}_{Q^{n+1}}\Big).}

We conclude that both inductive limits are topologically isomorphic to each other.

Now consider, for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, coset representatives
h_{1},\ldots,h_{s_{n+1}}
of
G_{0}/P^{n+1}
and
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{n}}
of
G_{0}/P^{n}
such that
h_{j}
gets mapped to
g_{i(j)}
under
G_{0}/P^{n+1}\rightarrow G_{0}/P^{n}
.
Then the transition map
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\rightarrow{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n+1}}(V_{n+1})
is given by

\bigoplus_{i=1}^{s_{n}}g_{i}\bullet V_{n}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{j=1}^{s_{n+1}}h_{j}\bullet V_{n+1}\,,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{s_{n}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\longmapsto\sum_{j=1}^{s_{n+1}}h_{j}\bullet v_{i(j)}.

Therefore it is injective and compact as the finite direct sum of compact homomorphisms, by
Lemma

A.3
(iii).
∎

Our next goal is to interpret
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})
as a subspace of
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
.
We know from
Proposition

1.5.4
that
{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\cong{\mathrm{Ind}}^{{\mathrm{la}},G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})
.
As each
V_{n}
is a BH-subspace of
V
, we consequently obtain injective continuous homomorphisms, for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle\iota_{n}\colon{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V_{n})\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V),

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{s_{n}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big[g\mapsto p^{-1}.v_{i}\text{ , for $g=g_{i}p$ with $p\in P^{n}$}\big],

Given
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
,
g\in G_{0}
, and
p\in P_{0}
, we write
f(g{\,\_\,}p)
for the locally analytic function

f(g{\,\_\,}p)\colon G_{0}\longrightarrow V\,,\quad h\longmapsto f(ghp).

We let
\mu(f)
denote
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
applied to a function
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
via the pairing (
1.26
).
The
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
-module action on
V
is denoted by
\mu\ast v
, for
v\in V
.
Also recall that
\dot{\mu}
signifies the involution from
Lemma

1.6.10
.

Lemma 3.2.5
.

The homomorphism

\iota\colon\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)

induced by the
\iota_{n}
is a closed embedding with

{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)=\left\{f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)\,\middle|\,\forall g\in G_{0},p\in P_{0},\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G):\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)=p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g)\right\}.

Proof.

It suffices to show the statement about
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
.
Indeed, then
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
is the intersection of the kernels of the continuous homomorphisms

(3.17)

C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)\longrightarrow V\,,\quad f\longmapsto\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)-p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g),

for
g\in G_{0},p\in P_{0},\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
.
As
V
is Hausdorff, these kernels are closed subspaces, and so is
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
.
Since
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
is of compact type by
Proposition

1.2.15
(iii),
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
is of compact type as well using
Proposition

A.4
.
Moreover, the induced homomorphism
\iota
is a continuous bijection onto its image.
Because
\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})
is the inductive limit of
K
-Banach spaces, we can apply a version of the open mapping theorem
[
11
, II. §4.6 Cor.]
to conclude that
\iota
is strict.

It remains to show the statement about
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
.
Let
\rho_{n}\colon P^{n}\rightarrow{\mathrm{GL}}(V_{n})
denote the representation (
3.14
) and
\rho_{n,v}\colon P^{n}\rightarrow V_{n}
, for
v\in V_{n}
, its locally analytic orbit maps.
First, consider
f\in{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)
.
Then there exists
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
f\in{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota_{n})
, i.e.
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V_{n})
and
f(gp)=p^{-1}.f(g)
, for all
g\in G_{0}
,
p\in P^{n}
.
Let
D_{n}\vcentcolon=1+{\pi}^{n}M_{d+1}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
, and note that
P^{n}=D_{n}\cdot P_{0}
.
It follows that

(3.18)

f(gxp)=(xp)^{-1}.f(g)=(\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})(xp),

for all
g\in G_{0}
,
x\in D_{n}
,
p\in P_{0}
.
We fix
g\in G_{0}
and
p\in P_{0}
for the moment and consider (
3.18
) as an identity of locally analytic functions in
x
on
D_{n}
.
The homomorphism

\displaystyle V_{n}\longrightarrow C^{\rm la}(P^{n},V_{n})\,,\quad v\longmapsto(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p),

is
P^{n}
-equivariant with respect to the left regular representation on
C^{\rm la}(P^{n},V_{n})
.
Therefore, it is equivariant for the
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
-action as well, and for
v=f(g)
, we obtain

(3.19)

(\rho_{n,\mu\ast f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p)=\mu\ast\big((\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p)\big),

for all
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
.
Finally, we apply
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
to (
3.18
) and compute for the functions restricted to
D_{n}
:

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\big((\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\dot{\mu}\ast\big((\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p)\big)\big)(1)

\displaystyle\text{, by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{Prop 1 - Compatibility of convolution and pairing for left regular representation} }

\displaystyle=\big((\rho_{n,\dot{\mu}\ast f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p)\big)(1)

\displaystyle\text{, by \eqref{Eq 3 - Equivariance of hyperalgebra-action on map that sends to orbit map}}

\displaystyle=p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g).

On the other hand, let
f\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)
such that
\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)=p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g)
, for all
g\in G_{0}
,
p\in P_{0}
, and
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
.
As
G_{0}
is compact, there exists some
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
f
factors over the BH-space
V_{n}
of
V
.
Moreover, we find
m\geq n
such that
f
is locally analytic with respect to the finite covering

(3.21)

G_{0}=\bigcup_{u\in G_{0}/P^{m}}\bigcup_{p\in P^{m}/D_{m}}upD_{m},

i.e.
f{\!\,\mid_{upD_{m}}}
is analytic, for all cosets
upD_{m}
.
By
Proposition

1.3.10
,
\rho_{n}
is a locally
K
-analytic map of locally
K
-analytic manifolds.
After increasing
m
, we therefore may assume that
\rho_{n}\circ{\mathrm{inv}}
is analytic on each coset of (
3.21
) as well.
In particular,
(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}}){\!\,\mid_{upD_{m}}}
is analytic, for all cosets
upD_{m}
and all
v\in V_{n}
.

To show that
f\in{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota_{m})
, we fix
g\in G_{0}
and
p\in P^{m}
and write
p=xp_{0}
, for
x\in D_{m}
,
p_{0}\in P_{0}
.
Then
f(g{\,\_\,}p_{0}){\!\,\mid_{D_{m}}}
and
(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0}){\!\,\mid_{D_{m}}}
are analytic, for all
v\in V_{n}
.
Indeed, let
u^{\prime}p^{\prime}D_{m}
by the coset of (
3.21
) containing
gp_{0}
.
Because
f
is analytic on
u^{\prime}p^{\prime}D_{m}
,
f(g{\,\_\,}p_{0})
is analytic on
g^{-1}u^{\prime}p^{\prime}D_{m}p_{0}^{-1}
.
But using that
D_{m}
is normal in
G_{0}
being the kernel of the reduction homomorphism, we see that this last set is equal to
D_{m}
.
Similarly
(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})
is analytic on the coset
g^{-1}u^{\prime}p^{\prime}D_{m}
so that
(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0})
is analytic on
D_{m}
.
If we apply
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
to the analytic function
(\rho_{n,v}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0}){\!\,\mid_{D_{m}}}
, we compute analogously to (
3.20
)

p_{0}^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g)=\mu\big((\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0})\big).

Combining this with the assumption
\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)=p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast f(g)
, we see that the functions on
D_{m}
satisfy

\displaystyle\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p_{0})\big)=\mu\big((\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0})\big),

for all
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
.
By
Proposition

1.6.12
, this implies that the locally analytic germs at
1
of
f(g{\,\_\,}p_{0})
and
(\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})({\,\_\,}p_{0})
agree.
As both functions are analytic on all of
D_{m}
, we conclude that they agree there, and we have, for all
g\in G_{0}
,
p=xp_{0}\in D_{m}\cdot P_{0}=P^{m}
:

f(gp)=f(gxp_{0})=(\rho_{n,f(g)}\circ{\mathrm{inv}})(xp_{0})=(xp_{0})^{-1}.f(g)=p^{-1}.f(g).

∎

Because
V
is of compact type, by
Proposition

1.2.15
(iii) we have the topological isomorphism
C^{\rm la}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\cong C^{\rm la}(G_{0},V)
induced by
f\otimes v\mapsto f({\,\_\,})\,v
.
Under this identification, the homomorphisms (
3.17
) are given by

C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\longrightarrow V\,,\quad f\otimes v\longmapsto\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)\,v-f(g)\,p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast v.

Therefore,
\iota
fits into the sequence of continuous homomorphism

(3.22)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(V_{n})\overset{\iota}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V

\displaystyle\overset{\psi}{\longrightarrow}\prod_{g\in G_{0},p\in P_{0},\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)}V

\displaystyle f\otimes v

\displaystyle\longmapsto\Big(\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)\,v-f(g)\,p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast v\Big)_{g,p,\mu}

which is algebraically exact, and
\iota
is strict.

We want to consider the strong dual of this sequence (
3.22
).
Note that by
[
67
, Rmk. 16.1 (ii)]
, the homomorphisms of this dualized sequence are continuous again.
By
[
67
, Prop. 9.11]
there is a topological isomorphism

\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in G_{0}\\
p\in P_{0}\\
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)\end{subarray}}V^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\Bigg(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in G_{0}\\
p\in P_{0}\\
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)\end{subarray}}\!\!V\,\,\Bigg)^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad\sum\ell_{g,p,\mu}\longmapsto\left[(v_{g,p,\mu})\mapsto\sum\ell_{g,p,\mu}(v_{g,p,\mu})\right].

Under this isomorphism, the transpose of
\psi
is given by

\displaystyle\psi^{t}\colon\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0},p\in P_{0},\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)}V^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\big(C^{\rm la}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\sum\ell_{g,p,\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left[f\otimes v\mapsto\sum\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)\,\ell_{g,p,\mu}(v)-f(g)\,\ell_{g,p,\mu}(p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast v)\right].

By applying
Corollary

1.4.5
at various points, we have

\displaystyle\mu\big(f(g{\,\_\,}p)\big)

\displaystyle=\mu\big[h_{1}\mapsto f(gh_{1}p)\big]

\displaystyle=\mu\big[h_{1}\mapsto\delta_{g}\big[h_{2}\mapsto\delta_{p}\big[h_{3}\mapsto f(h_{2}h_{1}h_{3})\big]\big]\big]

\displaystyle=\delta_{g}\big[h_{2}\mapsto\mu\big[h_{1}\mapsto\delta_{p}\big[h_{3}\mapsto f(h_{2}h_{1}h_{3})\big]\big]\big]

\displaystyle=(\delta_{g}\ast\mu\ast\delta_{p})(f).

Furthermore, by the definition of the contragredient action of
P_{0}
and
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
on
V^{\prime}_{b}
:

\ell_{g,p,\mu}(p^{-1}.\dot{\mu}\ast v)=(p.\ell_{g,p,\mu})(\dot{\mu}\ast v)=(\mu\ast p.\ell_{g,p,\mu})(v).

Moreover, both
V
and
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K)
are reflexive with their strong duals being reflexive Fréchet spaces.
Hence by
[
67
, Prop. 20.13]
and
[
67
, Cor. 20.14]
, we have a topological isomorphism

D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}\big(C^{\rm la}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}\,,\quad\delta\otimes\ell\longmapsto\big[f\otimes v\mapsto\delta(f)\,\ell(v)\big].

All in all, we see that the strong dual of (
3.22
) is the complex

(3.23)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{g\in G_{0},p\in P_{0},\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)}V^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\overset{\psi^{t}}{\longrightarrow}D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\iota^{t}}{\longrightarrow}\Big(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(V_{n})\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\longrightarrow 0

\displaystyle\sum\ell_{g,p,\mu}

\displaystyle\longmapsto\sum\delta_{g}\ast\mu\ast\delta_{p}\otimes\ell_{g,p,\mu}-\delta_{g}\otimes\mu\ast p.\ell_{g,p,\mu}.

As
\iota
is a closed embedding, the Hahn–Banach Theorem
[
67
, Cor. 9.4]
implies that
\iota^{t}
is surjective.
It follows from the open mapping theorem
[
67
, Prop. 8.6]
that
\iota^{t}
is strict.
Moreover, by
[
11
, IV. §4.1 Prop. 2]
we have
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})={\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}
where

{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}\vcentcolon=\big\{\ell\in D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\,\big|\,\forall v\in{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota):\ell(v)=0\big\}.

Since
{\mathrm{Im}}(\iota)^{\perp}={\mathrm{Ker}}(\psi)^{\perp}
by the algebraic exactness of (
3.22
),
Lemma

A.11
implies that
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})={\mathrm{Ker}}(\psi)^{\perp}\subset\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
.
As
{\mathrm{Im}}(\psi^{t})\subset{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})
and
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})
is closed, we conclude that
{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})=\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
.

Under the equivalence of
Proposition

1.4.10
(ii), the homomorphism
\iota^{t}
becomes a homomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules when
D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}
carries the
D(G_{0})
-module structure via multiplication on the left in the first factor.
We therefore obtain a topological isomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules

\Big(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(V_{n})\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\cong\big(D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\big)\big/{\mathrm{Ker}}(\iota^{t})\cong\big(D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}\big)\big/\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}.

The submodule
{\mathrm{Im}}(\psi^{t})
in turn is generated by the elements

\delta_{g}\ast\mu\ast\delta_{p}\otimes\ell-\delta_{g}\otimes\mu\ast p.\ell\quad\text{, for $g\in G_{0}$, $\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)$, $p\in P_{0}$, $\ell\in V^{\prime}_{b}$.}

Recall from Proposition
1.6.16
that
D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})
is generated by the elements of the form
\mu\ast\lambda
, for
\mu\in{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
,
\lambda\in D(P_{0})
.
Together with the density of the Dirac distributions, it follows that
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{}
is equal to the closure of the
D(G_{0})
-submodule generated by the vectors

\delta\ast\nu\otimes\ell-\delta\otimes\nu\ast\ell\quad\text{, for $\delta\in D(G_{0})$, $\nu\in D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})$, $\ell\in V^{\prime}_{b}$,}

where
V^{\prime}_{b}
is a separately continuous
D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})
-module via
Corollary

1.6.20
.
Using
Remark

1.5.9
we conclude the following:

Proposition 3.2.6
.

There is a canonical topological isomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules

\bigg(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(V_{n})\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}\cong D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.

3.3.
The Subquotients of
H^{0}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}
as Locally Analytic
{\mathrm{GL}}_{d+1}(K)
-Representations

On the level of locally analytic
G_{0}
-representations, the above
Proposition

3.2.6
already is a description of the term

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}\left(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}}_{Q^{n}}\right)

occuring in
Theorem

3.1.5
.
However, we want to extend this to a description as locally analytic
G
-representations or equivalently as
D(G)
-modules.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

There is a topological isomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-
D(P)
-bi-modules

D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}D(P)\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}D(G)\,,\quad\mu\otimes\nu\longmapsto\mu\ast\nu,

(cf.
[
73
, Lemma 6.1 (i)]
for the statement on the algebraic level).

For a separately continuous
D({\mathfrak{g}},P)
-module
M
, natural inclusion
D(G_{0})\hookrightarrow D(G)
induces a topological isomorphism

(3.24)

D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0}),\iota}\,}M\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P),\iota}\,}M\,,\quad\delta\otimes\ell\mapsto\delta\otimes\ell,

of
D(G_{0})
-modules.

Proof.

For (i), essentially the proof from
[
73
, Lemma 6.1 (i)]
for the algebraic statement applies:
The Iwasawa decomposition
G=G_{0}P
with
G_{0}\cap P=P_{0}
(see
[
16
, §3.5]
) gives a disjoint covering
G=\bigcup_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}G_{0}p
by compact open subsets.
In view of
Proposition

1.4.2
(iii) this yields a topological isomorphism

\displaystyle D(G)\cong\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(G_{0})\ast\delta_{p}

of
D(G_{0})
-
D(P)
-bi-modules, and one of
D(P_{0})
-
D(P)
-bi-modules

\displaystyle D(P)\cong\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(P_{0})\ast\delta_{p}.

Moreover, there is a topological isomorphism
[
46
, Lemma 1.2.13]

D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}\bigg(\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(P_{0})\ast\delta_{p}\bigg)\cong\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}D(P_{0})\ast\delta_{p}

of
D(G_{0})
-
D(P)
-bi-modules.
Passing to the quotients we obtain the topological isomorphism

\displaystyle D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}D(P)

\displaystyle\cong D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}\bigg(\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(P_{0})\ast\delta_{p}\bigg)

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}D(P_{0})\ast\delta_{p}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p\in P_{0}\backslash P}D(G_{0})\ast\delta_{p}\cong D(G).

of
D(G_{0})
-
D(P)
-bimodules.

For (ii), clearly the continuous homomorphism
D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\rightarrow D(G){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M
induces the continuous homomorphism (
3.24
) by passing to the homomorphism between the quotients

D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0}),\iota}\,}M\longrightarrow D(G){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P),\iota}\,}M

and completing.
Moreover, using the statement of (i) and
Lemma

1.5.11
(ii) we have a topological isomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules

D(G){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\cong\big(D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}D(P)\big){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\cong D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\,}\big(D(P){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\big).

Together with the well-defined continuous homomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules

\displaystyle D(G_{0})\otimes_{D(P_{0}),\iota}\big(D(P){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\big)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0}),\iota}\,}M,

\displaystyle\delta\otimes\lambda\otimes\ell

\displaystyle\longmapsto\delta\otimes\lambda.\ell.

we obtain
D(G){\,\otimes_{K,\iota}\,}M\rightarrow D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0}),\iota}\,}M
.
This homomorphism factors over the quotient as

D(G){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P),\iota}\,}M\longrightarrow D(G_{0}){\,\otimes_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0}),\iota}\,}M,

and one verifies that the completion of the latter homomorphism is an inverse to (
3.24
). ∎

Theorem 3.3.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}
be a
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
-equivariant vector bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d}
.
For the terms that occur on the right hand side of the description of the subquotients in
Theorem

3.1.5
, there are topological isomorphisms of
D(G)
-modules

\displaystyle\bigg(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}_{d-q}}\Big(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q}(\varepsilon_{n})}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}})^{\prime}_{b}\otimes_{K}v^{P^{n}_{d-q}}_{Q^{n}_{d-q}}\Big)\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\qquad\cong D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{d-q}),\iota}\,}\bigg(\widetilde{H}^{q}_{({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d-q})^{\mathrm{rig}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{K}^{d},{\mathcal{E}}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}\Big(v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)}_{B_{q}}\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\bigg),

for
q=1,\ldots,d
.
Here
P_{d-q}
acts via inflation from the subgroup
{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)
of its standard Levi factor
L_{d-q}
on
v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)}_{B_{q}(K)}
, and
{\mathrm{hy}}(G)
acts trivially there
10
10

10

Since
v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)}_{B_{q}}
carries the finest locally convex topology,
\Big(v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)}_{B_{q}}\Big)^{\prime}
equals the algebraic dual. The smooth dual of
v^{{\mathrm{GL}}_{q}(K)}_{B_{q}}
is a
K
-subspace thereof, but this inclusion is not an equality in general.
.

Proof.

We keep the simplified notation with
{r}=d-q\in\{0,\ldots,d-1\}
fixed and
{\mathrm{\bf P}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf P}}_{{d-q}}
,
{\mathrm{\bf Q}}\vcentcolon={\mathrm{\bf Q}}_{{d-q}}
.
Combining the topological isomorphism

\bigg(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}\cong D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}

from
Proposition

3.2.6
which was obtained via the topological embedding

\iota\colon\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\lhook\joinrel\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V

with the statement of
Lemma

3.3.1
(ii), already gives a topological isomorphism of
D(G_{0})
-modules

\omega\colon\Big(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\Big)^{\prime}_{b}\overset{\cong}{\longrightarrow}D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P),\iota}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.

It remains to show that
\omega
is
D(G)
-linear.

To do so we first construct a
G
-equivariant homomorphism

\tilde{\iota}\colon\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)

which is compatible with
\iota
and the restriction map

({\,\_\,}){\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}}\colon C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},V)\,,\quad f\longmapsto f{\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}},

in the sense that
({\,\_\,}){\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}}\circ\tilde{\iota}=\iota
.
Let
f\in{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})
correspond to the locally analytic function
f\colon G_{0}\rightarrow V_{n}
so that
f(gp)=p^{-1}.f(g)
, for all
g\in G_{0}
,
p\in P^{n}
.
We define an associated function
\tilde{f}\in C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)
as follows.
For
g\in G
with Iwasawa decomposition
g=g_{0}p
, with
g_{0}\in G_{0}
,
p\in P
, we set

\tilde{f}(g)=p^{-1}.f(g_{0})\in V

where
p^{-1}
acts on
f(g_{0})\in V
as explained in
Remark

3.2.2
.
This gives a well-defined function
\tilde{f}\colon G\rightarrow V
, because, for a different decomposition
g=g^{\prime}_{0}p^{\prime}
with
g^{\prime}_{0}=g_{0}p^{-1}_{0}
,
p^{\prime}=p_{0}p
, for some
p_{0}\in P_{0}
, we have

\tilde{f}(g^{\prime}_{0}p^{\prime})=(p^{\prime})^{-1}.f(g^{\prime}_{0})=p^{-1}.p_{0}^{-1}.f(g_{0}p^{-1}_{0})=p^{-1}.f(g_{0})=\tilde{f}(g)

as
p_{0}\in P^{n}
.

The function
\tilde{f}
is locally analytic:
For fixed
g=g_{0}p
with
g\in G_{0}
,
p\in P
, we consider the open neighbourhood
G_{0}p
of
g
.
There
\tilde{f}{\!\,\mid_{G_{0}p}}\colon hp\mapsto\tilde{f}(hp)=p^{-1}.f(h)
is locally analytic by
Proposition

1.2.14
(i) since
f
is locally analytic.
In total we obtain the sought homomorphism

\tilde{\iota}\colon\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\,,\quad f\longmapsto\tilde{f},

with
({\,\_\,}){\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}}\circ\tilde{\iota}=\iota
.

Next we want to show that
\tilde{\iota}
is
G
-equivariant with respect to the left-regular
G
-action
11
11

11

As
G
is not compact, the left-regular
G
-representation is not locally analytic but it is continuous nevertheless.
on
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)
.
To this end, let
f\in{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{m}}(V_{m})
be given by
\sum_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}
so that
f(g_{i}p)=p^{-1}.v_{i}
, for
p\in P^{m}
.
As usual
g_{1},\ldots,g_{s_{m}}
denote coset representatives of
G_{0}/P^{m}
.

We fix
g\in G
, and want to show that
\tilde{\iota}(g.f)=g.\tilde{\iota}(f)
.
Let
n\geq m
like in
Remark

3.1.4
so that
P^{n}/Q\subset p_{g,j}P^{m}/Q
with
p_{g,j}\vcentcolon=h_{j}^{-1}gg_{\sigma_{g}(j)}
.
Then we have seen that

g.f=g.\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{s_{m}}g_{i}\bullet v_{i}\bigg)=\sum_{j=1}^{s_{n}}h_{j}\bullet\tau_{p_{g,j}}(v_{\sigma_{g}(j)})\in{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n}).

Now consider
h\in G
, and let
j\in\{1,\ldots,s_{n}\}
such that
h\in h_{j}P^{n}/P
, i.e.
h=h_{j}p_{(n)}p
, for some
p_{(n)}\in P^{n}
,
p\in P
.
We compute that

\displaystyle\big(\tilde{\iota}(g.f)\big)(h)

\displaystyle=\big(\tilde{\iota}(g.f)\big)(h_{j}p_{(n)}p)=p^{-1}.\big((g.f)(h_{j}p_{(n)})\big)

\displaystyle=p^{-1}.p_{(n)}^{-1}.\tau_{p_{g,j}}(v_{\sigma_{g}(j)})=(\tau_{p^{-1}}\circ\tau_{p_{(n)}^{-1}}\circ\tau_{p_{g,j}})(v_{\sigma_{g}(j)}).

On the other hand, we have

g^{-1}h=(g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}p_{g,j}^{-1}h_{j}^{-1})(h_{j}p_{(n)}p)=g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}p^{-1}_{g,j}p_{(n)}p.

Hence

\displaystyle\big(g.\tilde{\iota}(f)\big)(h)

\displaystyle=\tilde{\iota}(f)(g^{-1}h)=\tilde{\iota}(f)(g_{\sigma_{g}(j)}p^{-1}_{g,j}p_{(n)}p)

\displaystyle=\big(p_{g,j}^{-1}p_{(n)}p\big)^{-1}.f(g_{\sigma_{g}(j)})=(\tau_{p^{-1}}\circ\tau_{p_{(n)}^{-1}}\circ\tau_{p_{g,j}})(v_{\sigma_{g}(j)})

This shows that indeed
\tilde{\iota}
is
G
-equivariant.

We now use the injective continuous integration homomorphism from
Proposition

1.4.7
(i) together with
[
67
, Cor. 18.8]
to obtain

C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\longrightarrow{\mathcal{L}}_{b}(D(G),V)\cong D(G)^{\prime}_{b}{\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}V.

As
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)
is reflexive by
Corollary

1.2.16
this yields an injective continuous homomorphism

\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}\,{\mathrm{Ind}}^{G_{0}}_{P^{n}}(V_{n})\overset{\tilde{\iota}}{\longrightarrow}C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,V)\longrightarrow C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}V

that we continue to call
\tilde{\iota}
.

Moreover, let
G=\bigcup_{i\in I}g_{i}G_{0}
be a disjoint covering, for coset representatives
g_{i}
of
G/G_{0}
, so that
C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K)\cong\prod_{i\in I}C^{\mathrm{la}}(g_{i}G_{0},K)
and
D(G)\cong\bigoplus_{i\in I}D(g_{i}G_{0},K)
.
Then there are topological isomorphisms

\displaystyle\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\cong\bigg(\prod_{i\in I}C^{\mathrm{la}}(g_{i}G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\bigg)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\text{, by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{BreuilHerzig18TowardsFinSlopePartGLn}{}{}, Lemma 2.1 (iii)]}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i\in I}\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(g_{i}G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\text{, by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Schneider02NonArchFunctAna}{}{}, Prop.\ 9.11]}}

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{i\in I}D(g_{i}G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\text{, by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Emerton17LocAnVect}{}{}, Prop.\ 1.1.32 (ii)]}}

\displaystyle\cong D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\iota}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.

\displaystyle\text{, by \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Kohlhaase05InvDistpAdicAnGrp}{}{}, Cor.\ 1.2.14]}}

The resulting
G
-equivariant isomorphism fits into the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\iota}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

where the horizontal homomorphism on the top is the transpose of
({\,\_\,}){\!\,\mid_{G_{0}}}
, and the bottom one is induced by the embedding
D(G_{0})\hookrightarrow D(G)
.
We finally arrive at the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G_{0}){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P_{0})}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G_{0},K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Big(\varinjlim_{n\in{\mathbb{N}}}{\mathrm{Ind}}_{P^{n}}^{G_{0}}(V_{n})\Big)^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(C^{\mathrm{la}}(G,K){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\pi}\,}V\big)^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{K,\iota}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(G){\,\widehat{\otimes}_{D({\mathfrak{g}},P),\iota}\,}V^{\prime}_{b}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{t}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}^{t}}

Since
\iota^{t}
is surjective, so is
\tilde{\iota}^{t}
.
It follows that the induced topological isomorphism
\omega
is
G
-equivariant because all the other homomorphisms in the lower “square” are.
We conclude that
\omega
is
D(G)
-linear by using the density of the Dirac distributions in
D(G)
.
∎

3.4.
The Functors
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}^{G}
of Orlik–Strauch

In this section we want to relate our description from
Theorem

3.3.2
to the functors
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}^{G}
introduced by Orlik and Strauch in
[
58
]
.
To this end we suppose that the non-archimedean local field
K
is of mixed characteristic, i.e. a finite extension of
{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
.
We begin by recapitulating the definition of the functors
{\mathcal{F}}_{P}^{G}
, but for simplicity only under the assumption that the field of definition
L
agrees with the field of coefficients
K
.
We normalize the absolute value of
K
such that
{\lvert{p}\rvert}=p^{-1}
.

Let
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
be a connected split reductive group over
K
.
We fix a split maximal torus and a Borel subgroup
{\mathrm{\bf T}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf B}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf G}}
, as well as a standard parabolic subgroup
{\mathrm{\bf P}}\supset{\mathrm{\bf B}}
with Levi decomposition
{\mathrm{\bf P}}={\mathrm{\bf L}}_{\mathrm{\bf P}}{\mathrm{\bf U}}_{\mathrm{\bf P}}
with
{\mathrm{\bf T}}\subset{\mathrm{\bf L}}_{\mathrm{\bf P}}
.
We assume that
{\mathrm{\bf G}}
and the above subgroups already are defined over
{\mathcal{O}}_{K}
.
Let
G={\mathrm{\bf G}}(K)
,
P={\mathrm{\bf P}}(K)
, etc. denote the associated locally
K
-analytic Lie groups and write
G_{0}={\mathrm{\bf G}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
,
P_{0}={\mathrm{\bf P}}({\mathcal{O}}_{K})
, etc. by abuse of notation.
Furthermore let
